# CAUSASEAL code audit — raw findings (2026-10-16)

> **Status: UNVERIFIED.** These are raw outputs from 12 read-only audit agents that each ran the code (node scripts, curl against `server.js`, MCP client calls) against `main` @ `9f05d44` (v0.10.10). They have **not** been through the planned reproduce/skeptic verification pass. Whoever fixes an item must reproduce it first and drop it if it does not reproduce. Some items are duplicates across lenses (marked in the index by shared root cause in the delegation brief).

## Summary

| Severity | Count |
| --- | --- |
| critical | 2 |
| high | 18 |
| medium | 75 |
| low | 54 |
| **total** | **149** |

Baseline on `main`: `npm test` 27/27 pass · `npx tsc --noEmit` clean · `npm run lint` 2 errors (react-hooks/set-state-in-effect in `connectors-http-view.tsx:63`, `connectors-mcp-view.tsx:39`) · `npm run build` OK · `npm audit` 27 vulns (1 critical: `next` 16.3.4 < 16.3.8).

### Lenses completed (12 of 38 planned)

- **xcfs-invariants** (11) — I read engine/xcfs.mjs end to end with its consumers (gateway, memory seeds, ops-agent harness, sermg, service/MCP, server.js) and confirmed behaviour with node probes against the real engine plus live HTTP/MCP calls (scratch state only; repo data/ untouched; my server process was stopped). Solid parts: reductionRatio can never divide by zero or produce NaN, node labels in expand() match the three memory seeds exactly, the redacted note does not re-trigger the directive cue, and MCP intercept/analyze correctly reject string-typed booleans via the JSON-schema validator. Not solid: the directive invariant is a 9-token substring regex with no Unicode normalisation and a prompt-suppression rule that compares "any verb" rather than "same verb", which yields a confirmed 100%-confidence INTERVENE on benign text ("sender") through seed X-CFS-003 and a confirmed INTERVENE→VERIFY downgrade of the judge-proof mutated shape via a single diacritic or a prompt that says "أرسل"; the HTTP routes and MCP run_agent.body never apply IncidentInputSchema, so string flags silently invert sensitiveData/trustedDestination; and reductionRatio is a function of how many steps the caller passes because step nodes are hard-coded non-critical.
- **gateway-correctness** (14) — Audited engine/gateway.mjs analyze()/fallback() by reading the full gateway, xcfs, memory, telemetry, persist and contracts modules plus every caller (server.js, connectors/service.mjs, mcp-server.mjs, ops-agent, sermg, UI), then confirmed behaviour with scratch scripts (CAUSASEAL_STATE_PATH isolated), a mocked global fetch for the OpenAI path, and live curl against server.js on :4100 and the /mcp endpoint. Solid: the structural rules in fallback() are clear and keyword-free, the result object always passes AnalysisResultSchema, the rules path is fast, the MCP SDK rejects string booleans and bad environments, and the four judge-proof cases (mutated/known/lookalike/partial) behave as documented. Not solid: the HTTP boundary applies no validation so stringified booleans fail open; a single DIRECTIVE regex hit escalates to a 100%-confidence INTERVENE via the one-invariant seed X-CFS-003 (with a fabricated cross-context claim driven by the 'enterprise' default); lastAnalysis is a process-global shared across orgIds; confidence/evidenceStrength/matchScore carry three different numbers for the same decision; and the OpenAI path has no timeout, trusts the model's matchedSignature, and auto-stores unbounded empty-invariant fingerprints. A null JSON body also hangs every POST endpoint (server-side sentinel collision, confirmed).
- **memory-matching** (14) — Lens: engine/memory.mjs fingerprint store and Jaccard matching, exercised with node scripts against a scratch CAUSASEAL_STATE_PATH and a server.js instance on :4102. Solid: jaccard() itself is correct (dedupes via Set, handles empty sets), matchByInvariants honours the 0.6 operating point and per-org buckets for stored fingerprints, matches counters are not shared across orgs, and ids never collide within a single healthy process. Not solid: storeFingerprint has zero input validation, so one unauthenticated POST /api/fingerprints with nodes:"oops" is persisted and turns every analyze/intercept/agent-run/SERMG/MCP intercept for the default demo org into a 500 that survives restart; loadFingerprints shallow-copies SEEDS so matchByInvariants (even with record:false) mutates the shared coveredEnvironments array and leaks environments into every other org; immunizedEnvironments and crossContext/learnedIn are derived from illustrative seeds and so report "learned in dev, blocked in enterprise" and 3/3 immunized environments for orgs that never learned anything; graphScore is computed but never used and is inverted by the seed node shape; no dedupe, no cap, length-based ids that collide across processes, non-atomic persistence, locale display strings stored as data, and search without trim/Arabic normalization. The "cross-context after learning" test passes without the learning step. Not reported as findings (other lenses): gateway's confidence floor Math.max(0.9, match.score) on a 0.75 Jaccard match.
- **mcp-protocol-conformance** (13) — Checked connectors/mcp-server.mjs against the installed @modelcontextprotocol/server 2.0.0 (README + dist typings + runtime), plus the /mcp wiring in server.js, by reading the SDK source and running an in-process MCP client, a heap/timing probe, and curl against server.js on :4111. Solid: fromJsonSchema is the correct non-deprecated 2.x schema path (the deprecated raw-shape overload is not used); all 10 tools have English titles, Arabic-then-English descriptions, full readOnly/destructive/idempotent/openWorld annotations, additionalProperties:false schemas that Ajv enforces into clean isError results; the "10 tools" claim in README/docs matches; Accept negotiation (406) is handled by the SDK; JSON-schema `default`s are not applied by Ajv (no useDefaults) but every handler tolerates undefined consistently with the advertised defaults. Not solid: (1) a new McpServer is built per HTTP request and each build recompiles 10 Ajv validators from fresh schema objects, which the SDK's shared default Ajv caches forever (~73 KB retained and ~10 ms per /mcp request, unauthenticated); (2) the five "read-only" tools append an MCP-READ event on every call, so a fresh org is never empty, list_events lists its own read, and get_report reports correctAllow=1 instead of the documented illustrative=true; plus a truncation path that emits invalid JSON, GET/DELETE /mcp reaching the stateless transport (GET opens a dangling SSE stream), and several low-severity conformance/hygiene items (hardcoded version 1.0.0, no instructions, no outputSchema, non-JSON-RPC parse error, CORS header inconsistency, duplicated tool catalog).
- **service-layer** (14) — I audited every exported function in connectors/service.mjs, traced each into its engine dependency (memory, telemetry, gateway, ops-agent, sermg, compliance) and its consumers (mcp-server.mjs, sdk.mjs, server.js, tests), ran npm test (27/27), and wrote four scratch probes (in-process, MCP client over server.js on :4110, curl over HTTP). Solid: the channel plumbing (http/mcp/sdk counters), the harness-vs-fingerprint intercept contract (MUTATED_LEAK → harness ALLOW / decision INTERVENE / redactedBody.sensitiveData=false), MCP input-schema enforcement of limit/offset/nodes types, and the English-title / Arabic-then-English description convention. Not solid: assertOrgId accepts `__proto__` (and server.js has no orgId validation at all), which pollutes Object.prototype through memory.mjs, leaks fingerprints across orgs and leaves /mcp returning 500 (RangeError in the MCP SDK's ajv traversal) for the rest of the process; formatResult's 25k truncation slices JSON mid-token, drops its own truncation marker and leaves structuredContent untruncated, and it triggers at list_events limit=50 because every page carries its rows twice (items + events); noteMcpRead runs before sessionSummary so every MCP read reports empty=false / illustrative=false / fromSession=true and a fabricated correctAllow=1; lastAnalysis is process-global so getSession leaks another org's last decision; untyped fingerprint fields are persisted and make analyze 500 for that org forever; run_sermg advertises count≤24 but the engine caps at 12; list_compliance ignores orgId; server.js re-implements service.mjs by copy-paste with weaker validation; and test/connectors-mcp.test.mjs writes the real data/causaseal-state.json on every npm test.
- **sdk-client** (12) — I read connectors/sdk.mjs end to end plus everything it touches (service.mjs, ops-agent.redactSensitive, server.js routing/CORS, telemetry connectorSdk, lib/connectors.ts snippet, the SDK page, tests, docs), then probed it with a scratch script against server.js on :4114 (scratch state path), a closed port, synthetic 500/400/503 servers, a text/html 200, a wrong-shape 200 and a non-responding server, and traced its import graph with a loader hook. Solid: the SDK is genuinely fail-closed (throws on unreachable and on non-2xx, VERIFY/unknown decisions yield body:null), sends no Authorization, the X-Causaseal-Channel header is honoured by server.js and counted under channels.sdk, and the three existing tests pass. Not solid: the INTERVENE 'redacted' body is a two-field overwrite that passes prompt/message/attachments through while the UI says it 'never returns the secret' (the one high); no timeout/AbortSignal; one Arabic-only error string that hides status and cause; no response validation; the HTTP intercept route bypasses service.interceptTool so SDK callers never get the named cut; orgId is validated on MCP but not on HTTP/SDK (arbitrary strings persisted); CORS preflight omits the SDK's own header; docs/snippet never state the fail-closed contract and have two identical branches; and none of the fail-closed behaviour is covered by tests. I did not touch the repository tree, used only the scratchpad for state, and stopped the server I started.
- **secrets-env-logging** (9) — Checked every process.env read in tracked files against .env.example, grepped tracked files and full git history for key/token/email/hostname patterns, inspected .gitignore coverage, the built out/ bundle for env leakage, and exercised server.js on :4121 with a bogus OPENAI_API_KEY and a scratch state path to observe what is logged, persisted and returned. Solid: no secrets or .env/state files in the tree or in any commit; OPENAI_API_KEY is only ever placed in the Authorization header and never logged; the AI-fallback console.error prints the Error object only (confirmed: "TypeError: fetch failed" + DNS cause, no request body); request payloads are not written to the state file (marker string absent); tests force rulesOnly so a developer's OPENAI_API_KEY cannot make npm test call OpenAI; the client bundle exposes only NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE; the SDK deliberately drops Authorization. Not solid: the optional OpenAI path ships the full unredacted incident to a third party with no timeout and no documentation; telemetry/compliance are not org-scoped (lastAnalysis leaks across orgIds); audit events carry only HH:MM:SS with no date and drop the reason the compliance panel claims is stored; /healthz discloses the absolute server path; malformed percent-encoded paths produce HTTP 500 plus a full stack trace in the log; .env.example is incomplete and nothing actually loads .env for server.js; scaffold auth/env residue (ChatGPT sign-in helpers, Sites/Wrangler env vars, placeholder electron metadata) remains in a public security repo.
- **deps-unused-scaffold** (9) — I grepped every dependency/devDependency against product source (app/, components/, lib/, engine/, connectors/, hooks/, server.js, test/, configs), built an import-reachability graph from the app pages, attributed all 27 npm-audit vulnerabilities to their top-level roots, and inventoried the vinext/Cloudflare/D1/Drizzle/Capacitor/Electron/brag scaffold. Solid: the engine/connector/server runtime deps (next, react, radix-ui, lucide-react, recharts, sonner, zod, @modelcontextprotocol/{server,node}, class-variance-authority, clsx, tailwind-merge, next-themes, cmdk, react-hook-form, @hookform/resolvers) are all genuinely used; @modelcontextprotocol/client is correctly dev-only (tests); vendor/shadcn-tailwind-4.13.0.css is live via globals.css; the missing optional peers (hono for @modelcontextprotocol/node, ajv for @hookform/resolvers) are never imported on the product path and are benign; `main: server.js` is correct for the Hostinger Express entry. Not solid: a dead vinext/Cloudflare/Drizzle devDependency cluster roots 16 of the 27 audit vulnerabilities (13 of 19 highs) while zero product files import it; ~30 KB of Sites installer scripts have no caller; eight packages exist only for 33 shadcn components no page reaches (date-fns has zero imports anywhere); the README's second half is the upstream starter README; package.json/lockfile still identify the repo as `site-creator-vinext-starter` 0.1.0 with no license/description/repository; 265 third-party audio binaries (16.6 MB, 53 % of tracked files) are committed with an undocumented music licence; and the documented Electron shell pins EOL Electron 26 and electron-updater 5.3.0 (CVE-2024-39698) with an auto-update publisher pointing at a repo URL that does not match origin.
- **react-views-runtime** (15) — Read every file under components/causaseal/*.tsx, app/layout.tsx, app/(dashboard)/layout.tsx and the providers, plus the lib/ helpers and chart components they call; reproduced the two eslint errors with npx eslint; started server.js on :4132 with a scratch state file and drove the four harness cases through /api/agent/run to verify what /api/session, /api/reports/metrics and /api/fingerprints really return to the views. Solid: hydration story is correct (LanguageProvider and ThemeToggle use useSyncExternalStore with server snapshots, next-themes handles the class pre-hydration, useSearchParams is wrapped in Suspense, no Date/locale calls in render paths); overview/reports/memory/sdk/connectors effects use a cancelled flag and clear their intervals; keys in architecture/impact/lab are unique. Not solid: the LanguageProvider reads localStorage unguarded (whole-app crash when storage is blocked, and there is no error boundary); live-data keys in overview feed and reports decisions collide on every harness compare (confirmed duplicates from the running server); monitor polling has a stale-response race; the sidebar memory badge is a hard-coded 3 and the memory page always shows the Illustrative badge even when the API says illustrative:false; the client-side compliance fallback claims "mapped" statuses the engine never proves; and the two lint errors have clean idiomatic fixes (cancelled-flag fetch; useSyncExternalStore for the device URL). Out of lens but visible to judges: most views (impact, monitor, overview, reports, lab, memory, investigate) are Arabic-only and ignore the language toggle while the shell switches to English; all fetches lack AbortController/timeouts and polls run while the tab is hidden.
- **api-client-lib** (12) — Audited lib/api/client.ts, lib/api/base.ts, lib/decisions.ts, lib/chart-session.ts, lib/compliance-client.ts (plus lib/seed-data.ts, lib/chart-demo.ts, lib/connectors.ts as they feed the same callers), cross-checked every response type against the live envelopes from server.js/engine on port 4131, and exercised the real client.ts (bundled with esbuild) under network-failure, HTTP-500, corrupt-localStorage and no-window conditions. Solid: apiUrl/NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE handling is correct and documented; all callers invoke the client from effects/handlers so static export prerender is safe; most response types match server.js field-for-field; chart-session helpers are pure and correct for server-shaped data. Not solid: the localStorage fallback in fetchFingerprints/createFingerprint fabricates a "saved to causal memory" success (with an ID that collides with seed X-CFS-001) when the API is unreachable, and because of a missing `await` the fallback is unreachable for HTTP errors; readJson discards the server's error envelope and does not check content-type so raw SyntaxErrors reach toasts on the judge-proof Architecture page; no fetch has a timeout/AbortSignal while three pages poll every 4 s; lib/compliance-client.ts contradicts the engine it claims to mirror and is shown on first paint of /reports; createFingerprint's loose contract lets the only caller drop environment/nodes so manually saved fingerprints are stored as "dev" with empty nodes; the exported zod schemas are never used to validate responses, and the SessionSummary type lies about lastAnalysis.confidence.
- **i18n-completeness** (12) — Checked lib/i18n.ts parity with a script (33 ar keys / 33 en keys, no missing keys either way; `as const` + `TranslationKey = keyof translations["ar"]` makes parity a compile-time guarantee and t() falls back to the key), and traced every useLanguage/NAV_I18N consumer, every Arabic-bearing file under app/, components/, lib/, the built out/*.html, the compiled Tailwind CSS, and the engine's date/time producers. Solid: the shell (sidebar, header, search dialog, filters, theme toggle) is fully bilingual; html lang/dir is switched in a useLayoutEffect and propagated to Radix DirectionProvider, the Toaster and the Sidebar side; architecture + the four connector pages use consistent `ar ? … : …` copy from bilingual catalogs (lib/architecture.ts, lib/connectors.ts). Not solid: the dictionary only covers chrome, so 8 of 13 routes ignore the toggle entirely (five views never call useLanguage, two are server components), the judge-facing Architecture page still leaks Arabic via formatDecision/HARNESS_DIFF in EN mode, three directional chevrons are inverted in both directions because `rtl:rotate-180` is applied to already-RTL glyphs, the engine persists locale-formatted ar-SA/en-GB date-time display strings instead of ISO, and the provider reads/writes localStorage unguarded inside the render snapshot.
- **a11y-semantics** (14) — Audited every component in components/causaseal plus the components/ui primitives they use, cross-checked against the static export (out/*.html, built CSS), Radix/recharts sources, a WCAG contrast script, and axe-core 4.13 run through jsdom on six built routes. Solid: all icon-only header buttons carry localized aria-labels, lucide icons default to aria-hidden, decision badges (ALLOW/VERIFY/INTERVENE) always carry text and are not colour-only, textareas and checkboxes are properly labelled via htmlFor, the Sheet/Dialog use Radix focus trapping with titles, every route has exactly one <h1>, and <html lang/dir> is kept in sync by the language provider. Not solid: Radix Select triggers, the Slider thumb and all Progress bars are unnamed (axe: button-name critical, aria-progressbar-name serious), light-theme success/warning/info badges fail AA contrast (3.3-4.3:1 at 12px), CommandDialog leaves a permanent English <h2>Command Palette</h2> before the <h1> on every page, five views stay Arabic while <html lang> says "en", there is no <nav> landmark or skip link, chart focus outlines are suppressed and most charts have no text alternative, and there is no reduced-motion handling anywhere. No server was started by this agent.

### Lenses NOT run (stopped by owner to save tokens)

persist-state, telemetry, ops-agent, sermg-mutations, contracts-drift, compliance-content, fixtures-judge-cases, mcp-e2e, connectors-catalog, server-http-security, server-static-routing, server-mcp-transport, api-e2e, dos-resilience, concurrency-multi-org, deps-vulns-runtime, build-deploy-config, tests-quality, lint-ts-strictness, code-duplication-structure, error-handling-consistency, jsdoc-engine-typing, perf-blocking, product-truth-claims, docs-drift, repo-hygiene. Several of these areas are partly covered by findings from neighbouring lenses (e.g. persistence via memory-matching, server hardening via secrets-env-logging).

## Details

### F-001 · [critical] storeFingerprint persists unvalidated nodes/tags/invariants; one POST breaks every analyze for the org (persistent 500)

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** security · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `server.js`, `connectors/service.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:236-259 storeFingerprint (lines 240, 246, 248); engine/memory.mjs:138-141 graphSimilarity; server.js:275-281 POST /api/fingerprints; connectors/service.mjs:325-350 storeFingerprintItem

storeFingerprint takes `nodes: input.nodes || []`, `tags: input.tags?.length ? input.tags : invariants` and `[...(input.tags || [])]` with no type checks, and server.js POST /api/fingerprints (no auth, orgId defaults to "demo") passes the raw JSON body straight in. A non-array `nodes` is written to the state file; on the next matchByInvariants call graphSimilarity does `nodes.map(...)` on it and throws, so analyze(), /api/gateway/intercept, /api/agent/run, /api/sermg/run and the MCP causaseal_intercept_tool all return 500 for that org, and because the row is persisted the breakage survives a server restart. The default org is the one judges use on the live URL. Non-iterable `tags` (e.g. a number) throws a TypeError at line 240 -> 500. Strings are spread into characters (invariants "abc" -> ["a","b","c"]), `environment` accepts any string although FingerprintSchema/contracts restrict it to cloud|enterprise|dev, and `{}` is accepted as a fingerprint with empty invariants that can never match.

**Proposed fix:** Add a StoreFingerprintInputSchema in engine/contracts.mjs (title non-empty string required; invariants/tags arrays of non-empty strings, max length; nodes array of {label,value,risk}; environment enum cloud|enterprise|dev; desc/confidence/date optional strings) and apply it in storeFingerprint (throw a typed ValidationError) with server.js mapping it to 400 and service.storeFingerprintItem reusing it. Defensively coerce persisted rows in loadFingerprints/listFingerprints (Array.isArray(nodes) ? nodes : []) so an already-poisoned state file cannot crash matchByInvariants, and make graphSimilarity guard non-array input.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Server on :4102 with scratch state. `curl -X POST /api/fingerprints -d '{"title":"poison","invariants":["zzz"],"nodes":"oops"}'` -> 201 {"nodes":"oops",...,"orgId":"demo"}. Then `POST /api/gateway/intercept` (demo) -> {"error":"Internal Server Error"} [500]; `POST /api/agent/run {"kind":"leak"}` -> 500; `POST /api/sermg/run` -> 500; MCP tools/call causaseal_intercept_tool -> {"isError":true,"text":"Error: nodes.map is not a function"}; same calls with orgId=clean -> 200. Server log: `TypeError: nodes.map is not a function at labels (engine/memory.mjs:140:11) at graphSimilarity (memory.mjs:141:37) at matchByInvariants (memory.mjs:199:28) at analyze (gateway.mjs:155:17)`. Restarted server with same state file: intercept demo still 500. `POST /api/fingerprints -d '{"title":"t","tags":5}'` -> 500, log: `TypeError: (input.tags || []) is not iterable at storeFingerprint (memory.mjs:240:89)`. Engine probe: storeFingerprint({title:"str",invariants:"abc",tags:"xyz"}) -> invariants ['a','b','c'], tags 'xyz'; storeFingerprint({},...) -> invariants [], tags []; environment "production-🚀" accepted and returned by immunizedEnvironments.
```

</details>

### F-002 · [critical] assertOrgId accepts `__proto__`/`constructor` and server.js never validates orgId: Object.prototype pollution leaks fingerprints across orgs and kills /mcp for the process lifetime

- **Lens:** service-layer · **Category:** security · **Breaking fix:** yes
- **Files:** `connectors/service.mjs`, `server.js`, `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/telemetry.mjs`
- **Location:** connectors/service.mjs:26 ORG_ID_PATTERN and :35-42 assertOrgId; server.js:16-18 orgFrom; engine/memory.mjs:87-94 orgBucket and :111-116 saveFingerprints; engine/telemetry.mjs:44-51 loadSession

ORG_ID_PATTERN is /^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/, so reserved property names pass validation. memory.mjs uses the orgId as a plain-object key: orgBucket does `if (!orgs[id]) orgs[id] = {}` and returns `orgs[id]`; for id="__proto__" that is Object.prototype, and saveFingerprints then executes `bucket.fingerprints = list`, i.e. Object.prototype.fingerprints = [...]. Every later org whose bucket is a fresh `{}` inherits that array (loadFingerprints checks `Array.isArray(bucket.fingerprints) && bucket.fingerprints.length`), so one request with orgId=__proto__ makes every new org see (and push into) the attacker org's fingerprint list, including titles/invariants/nodes. Any analyze that matches a seed (matchByInvariants → saveFingerprints) or stores a new fingerprint triggers it, so causaseal_analyze_path / causaseal_intercept_tool / causaseal_run_agent over MCP and POST /api/fingerprints or /api/analyze over HTTP are all entry points. The same pollution breaks the MCP SDK: every subsequent /mcp request fails inside @modelcontextprotocol/server's ajv `_traverse` with RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded, so the public MCP endpoint returns 500 until the process restarts. In addition, telemetry.loadSession("__proto__"|"constructor"|"toString") returns a non-array (Object.prototype / a function) and recordEvent throws `TypeError: current is not iterable`, surfacing as a raw tool error over MCP and a 500 over HTTP. server.js does not call assertOrgId at all (orgFrom = String(query||body||"demo")), so even a 5000-char or space-containing orgId is accepted with 200. The repo is public and judges may fuzz exactly these inputs.

**Proposed fix:** (1) In service.mjs add a reserved-name check to assertOrgId (reject `__proto__`, `constructor`, `prototype`, and any key where `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(Object.prototype, id)` or `id in Object.prototype`) and export it. (2) In server.js import assertOrgId and wrap orgFrom in try/catch returning 400 `{error}`. (3) Defence in depth in memory.mjs and telemetry.mjs: store org buckets in `Object.create(null)` objects (or a Map) and use `Object.hasOwn(orgs, id)` instead of truthiness; make loadSession return `Array.isArray(...) ? [...] : []`. (4) Add tests for `__proto__`/`constructor` orgIds on assertOrgId, memory, telemetry and the HTTP routes.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
In-process (service-layer-edge.mjs): `assertOrgId("__proto__") -> __proto__`, `assertOrgId("constructor") -> constructor`; `getSession({orgId:"__proto__",channel:"mcp"})` throws `TypeError - current is not iterable`; after `storeFingerprintItem({orgId:"__proto__",title:"poison"})`: `Object.prototype.fingerprints after: ARRAY(len=4)`, `({}).fingerprints leaked? -> true`, `brand-new org fingerprints total_count: 4 ... titles include poison? true`. service-layer-proto-inproc.mjs: `analyzePath({...MUTATED_LEAK, orgId:"__proto__"})` threw after 12 ms but left `Object.prototype.fingerprints: ARRAY len 3, X-CFS-001.matches=1, orgId=__proto__`; a fresh org then reported `matches: 1 orgId: __proto__`; loading the MCP SDK afterwards crashed in zod v4 util.js:722 `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'get')`. End-to-end over server.js :4110 (service-layer-run.sh): `GET /api/session?orgId=bad%20id -> 200`, 5000-char orgId `-> 200`, `GET /api/session?orgId=__proto__ -> 500`, `GET /api/events?orgId=toString -> 500`, `GET /api/reports/metrics?orgId=constructor -> 500`; `MCP alive before: OK`; `POST /api/fingerprints {orgId:"__proto__",title:"POISON-TITLE"} -> 201`; `fresh org after: count 4 titles ...|POISON-TIT orgIds __proto__ illustrative false`; `MCP alive after: FAILED: Error POSTing to endpoint: {"error":"Internal Server Error"}`; server log: `[CAUSASEAL] Request error RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded at _traverse (@modelcontextprotocol/server/dist/ajvProvider-...mjs:1915:20)`, plus `TypeError: events.filter is not a function` and `TypeError: loadSession is not a function or its return value is not iterable`.
```

</details>

### F-003 · [high] createFingerprint/fetchFingerprints localStorage fallback fabricates a 'saved to causal memory' success and mislabels data when the API is down

- **Lens:** api-client-lib · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `lib/api/client.ts`, `components/causaseal/investigate-view.tsx`, `components/causaseal/memory-view.tsx`
- **Location:** lib/api/client.ts:34-45 fetchFingerprints, lib/api/client.ts:47-75 createFingerprint; investigate-view.tsx:147-158 handleSaveFingerprint

When fetch() rejects (server.js not running — the UI's own toasts anticipate this: 'تأكد أن الخادم يعمل: npm start'), createFingerprint catches the error, writes a fingerprint into localStorage under key causaseal_fingerprints and resolves successfully. The caller then shows toast.success(`تم حفظ ${fingerprint.id} في الذاكرة السببية`) — a false statement: nothing reached engine/memory.mjs, the gateway will never match against it, and the generated id `X-CFS-${existing.length+1}` collides with seed X-CFS-001..003. fetchFingerprints' fallback then returns that local list with `illustrative: true` (it is user-created, not illustrative) and omits the seeds; once the server is back the local entries are never merged and silently vanish. The fallback also builds a Fingerprint without `environment`, `nodes`, `coveredEnvironments`, `origin` (schema drift vs storeFingerprint), uses `JSON.parse` unguarded (corrupt storage throws SyntaxError out of the catch), and references `localStorage` without a `typeof window` guard (ReferenceError in Node/SSR), unlike lib/connectors.ts:126 which guards. For a hackathon prototype whose core claim is 'remember why a past path was dangerous', a client that pretends to remember is the wrong failure mode.

**Proposed fix:** Remove the silent localStorage write path from createFingerprint and let the error propagate (the caller already has a toast.error branch). If an offline cache is wanted, make it explicit: return a discriminated union such as `{ fingerprint, persisted: false, source: "local" }`, prefix local ids (e.g. `LOCAL-001`) so they cannot collide with engine ids, have the caller show a distinct warning toast, and keep `illustrative` for seeds only. Guard every localStorage access with `typeof window !== "undefined"` and wrap JSON.parse in try/catch.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Bundled lib/api/client.ts with esbuild and ran it with a stub fetch that rejects: `createFingerprint resolved OK, id = X-CFS-001 | localStorage now: [{"id":"X-CFS-001","title":"t1",...` and `fetchFingerprints resolved: {"fingerprints":[{"id":"X-CFS-001",...}],"illustrative":true}`. With corrupt storage: `fetchFingerprints threw: SyntaxError Expected property name or '}' in JSON`. Without localStorage: `ReferenceError localStorage is not defined`. Code: client.ts:57-74 `catch { const existing = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem("causaseal_fingerprints") || "[]") ... localStorage.setItem(...); return { fingerprint } }`; investigate-view.tsx:158 `toast.success(\`تم حفظ ${fingerprint.id} في الذاكرة السببية\`)`. Engine seeds X-CFS-001..003 at engine/memory.mjs:8-66.
```

</details>

### F-004 · [high] Dead vinext/Cloudflare toolchain roots 13 of the 19 high npm-audit vulnerabilities while no product file imports it

- **Lens:** deps-unused-scaffold · **Category:** dependency · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `vite.config.ts`, `build/sites-vite-plugin.ts`, `build/sites-vite-plugin.LICENSE`, `cloudflare-env.d.ts`, `.openai/hosting.json`, `scripts/run-framework.mjs`, `scripts/execution-profile.mjs`, `scripts/sites-env.mjs`, `scripts/sites-env.sh`, `scripts/build-verified.sh`, `tsconfig.json`, `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md`
- **Location:** package.json devDependencies: vinext, vite, wrangler, @cloudflare/vite-plugin, @cloudflare/workers-types, @vitejs/plugin-react, @vitejs/plugin-rsc, react-server-dom-webpack; package.json overrides.miniflare; scripts.dev:vinext; tsconfig.json compilerOptions.types

Eight devDependencies exist only to run the upstream vinext-on-Cloudflare starter (vite.config.ts wires vinext() + the vendored OpenAI Sites plugin + @cloudflare/vite-plugin with Miniflare D1/R2 bindings). Nothing under app/, components/, lib/, engine/, connectors/, server.js or test/ imports any of them; the only reachers are vite.config.ts, build/sites-vite-plugin.ts, scripts/run-framework.mjs and the `dev:vinext` script. @vitejs/plugin-react, @vitejs/plugin-rsc and react-server-dom-webpack are listed as direct devDependencies solely because vinext declares them as peers. The project's own docs already say this layer is 'not on the product path' (CLAUDE.md:41) and should be removed 'in a later cleanup' (docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md:306), yet it is what a judge running `npm audit` sees first: it is the root of 16 of the 27 reported vulnerabilities (13 high: vinext, vite, wrangler, @cloudflare/vite-plugin, react-server-dom-webpack, miniflare, sharp, undici, ws, image-size, vite-plugin-commonjs, vite-plugin-dynamic-import, plus braces/fast-glob/micromatch shared with eslint-config-next). It also forces `overrides.miniflare.sharp`, drags Node >=22-only engines into a package that claims `>=20.9`, and makes every clone's `npm install` run workerd/miniflare/sharp postinstall scripts for tooling nobody runs. docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md:42 still describes the framework as 'Next.js 16.3.4 + vinext 1.0.0-beta.5 + @cloudflare/vite-plugin', which is false for the shipped product.

**Proposed fix:** Remove from package.json devDependencies: vinext, vite, wrangler, @cloudflare/vite-plugin, @cloudflare/workers-types, @vitejs/plugin-react, @vitejs/plugin-rsc, react-server-dom-webpack; delete `overrides.miniflare` and the `dev:vinext` script. Delete vite.config.ts, build/ (both files), cloudflare-env.d.ts, .openai/hosting.json, scripts/run-framework.mjs, scripts/execution-profile.mjs, scripts/sites-env.mjs, scripts/sites-env.sh, scripts/build-verified.sh. Change tsconfig.json `types` to `["node"]`. Regenerate package-lock.json (`npm install --package-lock-only`), then re-run `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run lint`, `npm test`, `npm run build` and `npm audit` and record the before/after vuln counts in the PR. Update docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md:42 and :306 to say the layer was removed. Retain: next, eslint-config-next (product toolchain).

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Usage sweep (scratch script grepping import/require specifiers): `dev  vinext prod=0 | scaf=3 vite.config.ts,scripts/install-ci.mjs`, `dev  vite prod=0 | scaf=2 vite.config.ts,build/sites-vite-plugin.ts`, `dev  wrangler prod=0 | scaf=3 scripts/sites-env.mjs`, `dev  @cloudflare/vite-plugin prod=0 | scaf=1 vite.config.ts`, `dev  @cloudflare/workers-types prod=0 | scaf=1 tsconfig.json`, `dev  @vitejs/plugin-react prod=0 | scaf=0`, `dev  @vitejs/plugin-rsc prod=0 | scaf=0`, `dev  react-server-dom-webpack prod=0 | scaf=0`. `npm ls react-server-dom-webpack` → only dependents are `@vitejs/plugin-rsc@0.5.26` and `vinext@1.0.0-beta.5`; same for @vitejs/plugin-rsc and @vitejs/plugin-react (vinext only). `npm audit --json` attribution script: `scaffold-only count: 19 of 27`; high rows rooted only in this cluster: @cloudflare/vite-plugin, image-size(vinext), miniflare, react-server-dom-webpack, sharp, undici, vinext, vite, vite-plugin-commonjs, vite-plugin-dynamic-import, wrangler, ws; mixed: braces/fast-glob/micromatch `roots=eslint-config-next|vinext`. vite.config.ts line 1-5: `import vinext from "vinext"; import { defineConfig } from "vite"; import hostingConfig from "./.openai/hosting.json"; import { readExecutionProfile } from "./scripts/execution-profile.mjs"; import { sites } from "./build/sites-vite-plugin";`. tsconfig.json: `"types": ["node", "@cloudflare/workers-types"]` and `"include": ["**/*.ts", ...]` so these files are type-checked. CLAUDE.md:41: 'the vinext/Cloudflare files (`vite.config.ts`, `.openai/`, `app/chatgpt-auth.ts`, `scripts/sites-env.*`) come from the upstream starter scaffold and aren't on the product path.' docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md:306: 'not used by the Hostinger target; leave in place but stop depending on it, or remove in a later cleanup'. Engines from node_modules: vinext `{"node":">=22"}`, wrangler `{"node":">=22.0.0"}`, vite `{"node":"^20.19.0 || >=22.12.0"}`.
```

### F-005 · [high] HTTP analyze/intercept accept stringified booleans and fail open: "false" destination treated as trusted, "false" sensitiveData treated as sensitive

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** security · **Breaking fix:** yes
- **Files:** `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/xcfs.mjs`, `engine/contracts.mjs`, `server.js`
- **Location:** engine/gateway.mjs:143-154 analyze(); engine/xcfs.mjs:50-59 deriveInvariants(); server.js:243-251, 284-295

analyze() and deriveInvariants() consume the raw HTTP body with no validation. sensitiveData and elevatedPrivilege are tested by truthiness (`if (body.sensitiveData)`), while the destination uses strict equality (`body.trustedDestination === false`). A client that sends JSON strings ("true"/"false" — common for form/query-string clients and some LLM tool callers) therefore gets inverted semantics: an untrusted destination ("false") is treated as trusted and the 'وجهة غير معتمدة' invariant is never derived, while a non-sensitive payload ("false") is treated as sensitive. The result is a fail-open decision on the exact shape the product is meant to catch. IncidentInputSchema in engine/contracts.mjs already declares these fields as z.boolean() but it is never applied at the HTTP boundary or inside analyze(); only the MCP SDK validates (confirmed: /mcp rejects with 'must be boolean'). preferRules has the same issue (`Boolean(options.rulesOnly || body.preferRules)` — the string "false" forces rules mode).

**Proposed fix:** Validate once at the entry of analyze() (or in server.js before calling it) with IncidentInputSchema.safeParse and return HTTP 400 with the zod issues on failure; in deriveInvariants/expand use strict `=== true` / `=== false` for all three flags via one normalized object so strings can never be coerced. Add a gateway test that string booleans are rejected (HTTP) and that `trustedDestination:false` always yields the destination invariant.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
curl POST /api/analyze with {"tool":"send_to_workspace","trustedDestination":"false","elevatedPrivilege":"false","sensitiveData":"false",...} => {decision:'VERIFY', matchedSignature:'PARTIAL-MATCH', invariants:['أداة إرسال','بيانات حساسة']}. Same body with real booleans false => {decision:'INTERVENE', matchedSignature:'X-CFS-002', invariants:['أداة إرسال','وجهة غير معتمدة']}. Script: deriveInvariants({sensitiveData:'false',trustedDestination:'false',elevatedPrivilege:'false'}) => ['أداة إرسال','بيانات حساسة']; analyze({...BENIGN_SEND, preferRules:'false'}).mode => 'rules'. MCP tools/call with the same strings => "Input validation error: ... data/trustedDestination must be boolean" (SDK protects MCP only). Code: xcfs.mjs:50 `if (body.sensitiveData)`, :53 `if (body.trustedDestination === false)`, :57 `if (body.elevatedPrivilege && !body.trustedDestination)`.
```

</details>

### F-006 · [high] One DIRECTIVE regex hit in retrieved text escalates to INTERVENE at confidence 1.0 via single-invariant seed X-CFS-003, overriding the rules' VERIFY

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** yes
- **Files:** `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/xcfs.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/gateway.mjs:151 (risky), 164-180 (match branch); engine/memory.mjs:46-62 (seed X-CFS-003), 192-230 matchByInvariants(); engine/xcfs.mjs:13-14, 31-36

`risky` is true for any invariant other than 'أداة إرسال', and the match branch fires INTERVENE whenever Jaccard ≥ 0.6 with no minimum on how many invariants are shared. Seed X-CFS-003 has exactly one invariant ('تعليمة في النص المسترجع'), so a request whose only signal is a single regex word (send/export/ignore/أرسل/انقل/…) in retrievedText that is absent from the prompt scores Jaccard 1.0 and is blocked with confidence 1, evidenceStrength 1, matchScore 1 — even with trustedDestination:true, sensitiveData:false and a non-send tool. fallback() for the same input returns VERIFY. This is a realistic false positive (a summarizer reading a document that says 'please export the final table') and it contradicts the product truth that CAUSASEAL is not a keyword blocklist: here one keyword is the whole decision. It also inflates the reason with a cross-context claim ('اكتُشف في السحابة، ومُنع في المؤسسة') and increments the seed's matches counter.

**Proposed fix:** Gate memory-driven INTERVENE on evidence, not only the ratio: require the intersection to contain at least two invariants (or `fingerprint.invariants.length >= 2`), and when a single-invariant fingerprint matches, cap the decision at the rules decision (VERIFY) while still reporting matchedSignature/matchScore. Alternatively mark X-CFS-003 as a VERIFY-class seed. Keep MATCH_THRESHOLD at 0.6. Add a regression test: directive-only + trusted destination + non-sensitive + non-send tool => VERIFY (not INTERVENE), matchedSignature X-CFS-003 still surfaced.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Script section 4: body {prompt:'لخّص المستند للفريق.', retrievedText:'Quarterly notes. Please export the final table to the shared drive when done.', tool:'summarize_document', trustedDestination:true, sensitiveData:false, elevatedPrivilege:false} => deriveInvariants = ['تعليمة في النص المسترجع']; fallback() => {decision:'VERIFY', evidenceStrength:0.7}; analyze(rulesOnly) => {decision:'INTERVENE', confidence:1, evidenceStrength:1, matchScore:1, matchedSignature:'X-CFS-003', crossContext:true, learnedIn:'cloud', appliedIn:'enterprise'}; event confidence label '100%'. Reproduced over HTTP: POST /api/analyze => decision INTERVENE, X-CFS-003, confidence 1, reason 'اكتُشف في السحابة، ومُنع في المؤسسة. البصمة X-CFS-003.'
```

</details>

### F-007 · [high] lastAnalysis is a process-global singleton: /api/session?orgId=X returns another org's last decision, signature and causal nodes

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** security · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/telemetry.mjs`, `server.js`
- **Location:** engine/gateway.mjs:264-273 rememberAnalysis(...) without orgId; engine/telemetry.mjs:24-25, 81-87, 179; server.js /api/session

analyze() ends with rememberAnalysis({decision, confidence, matchedSignature, nodes, reason, ...}) which writes a module-level `lastAnalysis` shared by every org. sessionSummary(orgId) then returns that global under `lastAnalysis`, and server.js spreads sessionSummary into GET /api/session, which the Overview page renders (components/causaseal/overview-view.tsx:77, 261). All other telemetry and fingerprint storage is bucketed per orgId and the test suite explicitly asserts org isolation ('org alpha fingerprints are invisible to org beta'), so this is a real cross-tenant leak of the last decision, matched signature, reason text and causal node values (which include the tool name) to any caller with any orgId.

**Proposed fix:** Key the snapshot by org: `const lastAnalysisByOrg = {}`; `rememberAnalysis(snapshot, { orgId })` stores `lastAnalysisByOrg[orgId]`; sessionSummary returns `lastAnalysisByOrg[orgId] ?? null`; gateway passes `{ orgId }`; resetTelemetryCache clears the map. Add a test that org B's sessionSummary().lastAnalysis is null after org A analyzes.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Script section 9: after analyze(NOVEL_LEAK, orgId:'tenant-a') then analyze(BENIGN_SEND, orgId:'tenant-b'): sessionSummary('tenant-a').lastAnalysis.decision => 'ALLOW' (tenant-a's own last was INTERVENE); sessionSummary('never-analyzed').lastAnalysis.decision => 'ALLOW'. Live server: curl 'http://127.0.0.1:4100/api/session?orgId=someone-else' => {orgId:'someone-else', eventCount:0, lastAnalysis:{decision:'INTERVENE', matchedSignature:'X-CFS-003', nodes:['نص مسترجع موجّه','تعليمة في المسترجع']}} — data from an analysis run under orgId 'http-kw'. Code: telemetry.mjs:25 `let lastAnalysis = null`, :81-83 `rememberAnalysis(snapshot){ lastAnalysis = snapshot }`, :179 `lastAnalysis,` inside sessionSummary(orgId).
```

</details>

### F-008 · [high] Per-request createCausasealMcpServer() recompiles 10 Ajv schemas and leaks ~73 KB per /mcp request via the SDK's shared Ajv cache

- **Lens:** mcp-protocol-conformance · **Category:** perf · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `connectors/mcp-server.mjs`, `server.js`
- **Location:** connectors/mcp-server.mjs: createCausasealMcpServer() -> fromJsonSchema({...}) literals at lines 146, 188, 250, 307, 351, 405, 528, 571, 664; server.js:224 `const mcp = createCausasealMcpServer()` inside the request handler

server.js calls createCausasealMcpServer() for every POST /mcp (initialize, notifications/initialized, tools/list, each tools/call). Each call passes a brand-new JSON-schema object literal to fromJsonSchema(). In @modelcontextprotocol/server 2.0.0, fromJsonSchema() immediately calls the module-level default validator's getValidator(schema), which does `engine.compile(schema)` on a single shared Ajv2020 instance; Ajv's `_addSchema` caches every compiled schema in a strong `Map` keyed by the schema object (`this._cache.set(sch.schema, sch)`). Because the schema objects are new on every request, the cache never hits and grows without bound for the lifetime of the process. /mcp has no auth by design, so any client can grow server memory with trivial requests, and every legitimate request pays ~10 ms of Ajv code generation before the tool even runs. On Hostinger's long-lived Node process this is a slow OOM / cheap remote resource-exhaustion vector.

**Proposed fix:** Hoist the ten `fromJsonSchema(...)` results (or at minimum the JSON-schema object literals) to module-level constants in connectors/mcp-server.mjs so Ajv's identity-keyed cache hits on every request (verified: reusing the same object adds ~0 bytes). Keep the per-request McpServer if desired (Protocol holds a single `_transport`, so a shared server is not safe for concurrent stateless requests) or move to the SDK's `createMcpHandler` per-request factory. Add a test that constructs N servers and asserts the registered tool `inputSchema` JSON objects are `Object.is`-identical across instances (or asserts bounded heap growth).

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
SDK: node_modules/@modelcontextprotocol/server/dist/src-CX2iR2pK.mjs:7588 `function fromJsonSchema(schema, validator) { const check = validator.getValidator(schema); ...` ; ajvProvider-CEoC__sr.mjs:7476 `getValidator(schema) { ... engine.compile(schema)` ; ajvProvider:3870-3881 `let sch = this._cache.get(schema); if (sch !== void 0) return sch; ... this._cache.set(sch.schema, sch);` ; index.mjs:1864 `fromJsonSchema$1(schema, validator ?? (_defaultValidator ??= new DefaultJsonSchemaValidator()))` (module singleton).
```
