### F-039 · [medium] Eight packages are imported only by 33 shadcn/ui components that no page reaches (date-fns has zero imports anywhere)

- **Lens:** deps-unused-scaffold · **Category:** dependency · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `package.json`, `components/ui/calendar.tsx`, `components/ui/carousel.tsx`, `components/ui/combobox.tsx`, `components/ui/drawer.tsx`, `components/ui/input-otp.tsx`, `components/ui/message-scroller.tsx`, `components/ui/resizable.tsx`, `eslint.config.mjs`
- **Location:** package.json dependencies: date-fns, @base-ui/react, @shadcn/react, embla-carousel-react, input-otp, react-day-picker, react-resizable-panels, vaul; components/ui/{accordion,alert-dialog,aspect-ratio,attachment,breadcrumb,bubble,button-group,calendar,carousel,combobox,context-menu,drawer,dropdown-menu,form,hover-card,input-otp,item,kbd,marker,menubar,message-scroller,message,native-select,navigation-menu,pagination,popover,radio-group,resizable,scroll-area,switch,tabs,toggle-group,toggle}.tsx

An import-reachability walk from every app/**/page.tsx and layout shows 33 of the vendored components/ui files are never imported, directly or transitively, by any route. Seven production dependencies are imported exclusively by those dead files: @base-ui/react (combobox.tsx), @shadcn/react (message-scroller.tsx), embla-carousel-react (carousel.tsx), input-otp (input-otp.tsx), react-day-picker (calendar.tsx), react-resizable-panels (resizable.tsx) and vaul (drawer.tsx). `date-fns` is imported by nothing at all in the repository; it is present only as a peer of @base-ui/react and a dependency of react-day-picker, both dead. Next tree-shakes them out of out/ (0 bundle hits), so this is install, lockfile, tsc and lint surface rather than shipped bytes - but it is exactly the 'dead code shipped in product path' a strict reviewer flags, and eslint.config.mjs widens rule exemptions over the whole components/ui/** tree to keep this unused registry lint-clean (its comment also says 'vendored verbatim from shadcn@4.17.0' while vendor/ ships shadcn-tailwind-4.13.0.css).

**Proposed fix:** Delete the 33 unreachable components/ui files and remove date-fns, @base-ui/react, @shadcn/react, embla-carousel-react, input-otp, react-day-picker, react-resizable-panels and vaul from package.json (any of them can be re-added later with `npx shadcn add <name>`). Re-run the reachability check, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`. Optionally narrow the eslint override to the files that remain and fix the version in its comment to match vendor/ (4.13.0) or whatever `components.json` was generated from.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Reachability script output: `REACHABLE from app/ pages: 88 of 120` followed by the 33 `components/ui/*.tsx` paths listed above, then `EXTERNAL PACKAGES imported ONLY by unreachable files: @base-ui/react, @shadcn/react, embla-carousel-react, input-otp, react-day-picker, react-resizable-panels, vaul`. Usage sweep: `dep  date-fns prod=0 | scaf=0 | scripts=` (zero imports); `dep  @base-ui/react prod=1 components/ui/combobox.tsx`; `dep  vaul prod=1 components/ui/drawer.tsx`; etc. `npm ls date-fns`: `+-- @base-ui/react@1.8.0 | `-- date-fns@4.4.0 deduped`, `+-- date-fns@4.4.0`, `` `-- react-day-picker@10.0.1 `-- date-fns@4.4.0 deduped``. Bundle check `grep -rl <pkg> out/_next/static | wc -l`: embla 0, input-otp 0, react-day-picker 0, resizable-panels 0, base-ui 0, @shadcn/react 0, date-fns 0. eslint.config.mjs: `files: ["components/ui/**/*.{ts,tsx}", "hooks/use-mobile.ts"], rules: { "@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars": "off", "react-hooks/purity": "off", "react-hooks/set-state-in-effect": "off" } // These files are vendored verbatim from shadcn@4.17.0`; vendor/ contains `shadcn-tailwind-4.13.0.css`.
```

### F-040 · [medium] Electron shell pins EOL Electron 26.6.10 and electron-updater 5.3.0 (CVE-2024-39698), with a GitHub auto-update publisher pointing at a repo URL that does not match origin

- **Lens:** deps-unused-scaffold · **Category:** security · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `electron/package.json`, `electron/package-lock.json`, `electron/electron-builder.config.json`
- **Location:** electron/package.json dependencies.electron-updater ^5.3.0, devDependencies.electron ^26.2.2, devDependencies.electron-builder ~23.6.0, repository.url; electron/electron-builder.config.json publish.provider

Capacitor/Electron is a documented feature (README 'دعم المنصات', build:mac/linux/win scripts), so the shell must stay - but its pins are from 2023 and one is vulnerable. The lockfile resolves electron 26.6.10 (Electron 26 reached end-of-life when 29 shipped and receives no Chromium security fixes), electron-updater 5.3.0 (GHSA-9jxc-qjr9-vjxq / CVE-2024-39698: signature-validation bypass in electron-updater < 6.3.0-alpha.6 on Windows, i.e. a malicious update can pass code-signing checks - the exact threat a 'build:win NSIS installer' pipeline creates), and electron-builder 23.6.0. electron/src/index.ts wires `autoUpdater` from electron-updater and electron-builder.config.json sets `"publish": {"provider": "github"}`, which derives owner/repo from package.json `repository.url` - but that URL is `github.com/aboalrejal-ai/causaseal` while the real remote is `github.com/aboalrejalai/causaseal`, so a packaged app would poll a non-existent repository for updates. The electron lockfile's root `name` is `CAUSASEAL` while electron/package.json says `causaseal-desktop`, showing the lock is stale relative to the manifest. Severity would be high if installers were distributed; README states they are not, hence medium.

**Proposed fix:** In electron/package.json bump `electron` to a currently supported major (e.g. ^33 or whatever @capacitor-community/electron 5.x supports), `electron-updater` to ^6.3.0 or later, `electron-builder` to ^25; fix `repository.url` to https://github.com/aboalrejalai/causaseal (or set explicit `publish.owner`/`publish.repo` in electron-builder.config.json); regenerate electron/package-lock.json so its root name matches; confirm `npm --prefix electron run build` still compiles. Document in README that desktop auto-update is only wired when a GitHub release exists.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
electron/package-lock.json resolved versions (node script over `packages`): `electron 26.6.10`, `electron-updater 5.3.0`, `electron-builder 23.6.0`, `electron-rebuild 3.2.9`, `@capacitor-community/electron 5.0.1`; `lockfileVersion 3 name CAUSASEAL`. electron/package.json: `"name": "causaseal-desktop"`, `"repository": {"type":"git","url":"https://github.com/aboalrejal-ai/causaseal"}`, `"electron-updater": "^5.3.0"`, `"electron": "^26.2.2"`, `"electron-builder": "~23.6.0"`. `git remote -v` → `origin https://github.com/aboalrejalai/causaseal (fetch)`. electron/electron-builder.config.json: `"publish": {"provider": "github"}`, `"win": {"target": "nsis"}`. electron/src/index.ts line 7: `import { autoUpdater } from 'electron-updater';`. README: 'npm run build:win # بناء حزمة Windows (NSIS Installer)' and 'Document Capacitor / Electron pack scripts without shipping store releases' (CHANGELOG:135).
```

</details>

### F-041 · [medium] package.json and lockfile still identify the project as site-creator-vinext-starter 0.1.0 with no description, license, repository or LICENSE file

- **Lens:** deps-unused-scaffold · **Category:** hygiene · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `package.json`, `package-lock.json`
- **Location:** package.json name/description/license/repository/author; package-lock.json name + version (root entry)

The public repo that judges will open has `"name": "site-creator-vinext-starter"` - the upstream template's name, not CAUSASEAL - and no `description`, `license`, `repository`, `author` or `homepage`; there is no LICENSE/COPYING file at the root (electron/package.json claims MIT, so the two manifests disagree). The lockfile root entry is `site-creator-vinext-starter@0.1.0` while package.json is 0.10.10: every release bump edited package.json only, and `npm ci` never rewrites the lock, so the first `npm install` a judge runs produces an unrelated lockfile diff. `"main": "server.js"` is fine (it matches the Hostinger 'Entry file' and is harmless with `"type": "module"`), and `"private": true` is correct; the problem is identity and licence metadata, which an automated reviewer scores directly and which GitHub surfaces in the repo header.

**Proposed fix:** Set `"name": "causaseal"`, add `"description"` (one line matching AGENTS.md 'causal decision layer above an agent tool harness'), `"repository": {"type":"git","url":"https://github.com/aboalrejalai/causaseal"}`, `"homepage": "https://causaseal.aboalrejal.com"`, `"author"`, and a `"license"` the owner chooses (then add the matching LICENSE file; align electron/package.json). Run `npm install --package-lock-only` so the lock root becomes causaseal@0.10.x, and add a release-rule note in .cursor/rules/changelog.mdc to refresh the lock on each version cut. Keep `private: true` and `main: server.js`.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
package.json lines 1-7: `"name": "site-creator-vinext-starter", "version": "0.10.10", "private": true, "engines": {"node": ">=20.9"}` and no description/license/repository/author keys anywhere in the file. package-lock.json head: `"name": "site-creator-vinext-starter", "version": "0.1.0", "lockfileVersion": 3`; root package entry (node script): `{"rootName":"site-creator-vinext-starter","rootVersion":"0.1.0"}`. `ls LICENSE* COPYING*` → 'No such file or directory'. electron/package.json: `"license": "MIT"`, `"author": {"name": "CAUSASEAL Team", "email": "contact@causaseal.sa"}`. `git log --oneline -3` shows `9f05d44 chore(release): cut 0.10.10` with no lockfile root change.
```

</details>

### F-042 · [medium] engines.node >=20.9 understates what the shipped scripts need, and .npmrc disables the checks that would reveal it

- **Lens:** deps-unused-scaffold · **Category:** build · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `package.json`, `.npmrc`, `README.md`
- **Location:** package.json engines.node; .npmrc legacy-peer-deps=true / engine-strict=false; README 'المتطلبات: Node.js >=20.9', Hostinger table 'Node version 22.x', vinext section '>=22.13.0'

package.json promises Node >=20.9 (and README repeats it), but the same package.json ships scripts whose dependencies require Node 22: `cap:sync`/`cap:open:*`/`build:android|ios` use @capacitor/cli (engines >=22.0.0), `dev:vinext` uses vinext (>=22) and wrangler (>=22.0.0), vite requires ^20.19 || >=22.12. Hostinger itself runs 22.x per the README table, so three different Node claims appear in one README. Nobody notices because .npmrc sets `engine-strict=false` and `legacy-peer-deps=true`, which also silences real peer conflicts in the tree: esbuild@0.25.12 (hoisted by drizzle-kit) is invalid against vite's `^0.27.0 || ^0.28.0`, and `npm ls` exits with ELSPROBLEMS. The `.npmrc` was inherited from the starter to make its sprawling toolchain install at all; once the scaffold is gone it only hides problems from the owner and from judges who run `npm ls`/`npm doctor`.

**Proposed fix:** After removing the vinext/Drizzle scaffold: set `"engines": {"node": ">=22"}` (what Hostinger runs and what the documented Capacitor scripts need) or, if 20.9 support matters, state it only for `npm start`; make README carry a single Node statement. Delete `legacy-peer-deps=true` from .npmrc (keep `engine-strict=false` only if a specific reason is documented), run `npm install` to confirm the tree resolves with no `invalid` peers, and have `npm ls` exit 0 as part of the PR verification.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
.npmrc: `legacy-peer-deps=true` / `engine-strict=false`. package.json: `"engines": {"node": ">=20.9"}`. node_modules engines: `@capacitor/cli {"node":">=22.0.0"}`, `wrangler {"node":">=22.0.0"}`, `vinext {"node":">=22"}`, `vite {"node":"^20.19.0 || >=22.12.0"}`, `@vitejs/plugin-react {"node":"^20.19.0 || >=22.12.0"}`, `next {"node":">=20.9.0"}`. README: 'المتطلبات: Node.js `>=20.9` و npm.'; Hostinger table 'Node version | **22.x**'; 'Node.js `>=22.13.0` (for the vinext/Sites path; CAUSASEAL Hostinger path uses `>=20.9`)'. `npm ls esbuild` → `esbuild@0.25.12 invalid: "^0.27.0 || ^0.28.0" from node_modules/vite` and `npm error code ELSPROBLEMS`. (ajv@6 vs @hookform/resolvers ^8.12 also appears, but `peerDependenciesMeta.ajv.optional === true` and the product imports only `@hookform/resolvers/zod`, so that one is benign.)
```

</details>

### F-043 · [medium] confidence, evidenceStrength and matchScore disagree for the same decision (0.9 floor vs 0.75 score vs '75%' label; rules INTERVENE has no confidence but evidenceStrength 0.9)

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** yes
- **Files:** `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/contracts.mjs`, `lib/api/client.ts`
- **Location:** engine/gateway.mjs:51-55 fallback(), 165-172 match branch (`confidence: Math.max(0.9, match.score)`), 220-223, 231-233, 239-244

Three numbers describe one decision with no documented relationship. In the match branch confidence is floored at 0.9 regardless of the actual Jaccard score, so a 0.75 match is reported as confidence 0.9 while evidenceStrength and matchScore say 0.75 and the live-feed label says '75%'. In the rules branch INTERVENE/VERIFY carry hard-coded evidenceStrength 0.9/0.7 with no confidence at all, so the event label and the auto-stored fingerprint's confidence are always '—' (the `typeof result.matchScore === 'number'` ternary at 220-223 is dead because `!match` implies matchScore is undefined). The 0.9 floor is exactly the kind of claim-beyond-evidence the AGENTS.md product-truth rule forbids, and a judge reading the API will see three inconsistent 'confidence' values. lib/api/client.ts:127 also types lastAnalysis.confidence as a required number although the engine sends undefined.

**Proposed fix:** Pick one semantics and document it in contracts.mjs: `matchScore` = Jaccard when matched (else absent); `confidence` = matchScore when matched, else a documented rules confidence (e.g. derived from how many of the three structural conditions fired); `evidenceStrength` either equals the share of fingerprint invariants present or is removed. Drop the Math.max(0.9, …) floor and the dead ternary at 220-223; make lib/api/client.ts lastAnalysis.confidence optional. Update the UI label to use the same field everywhere.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Script section 5 (MUTATED_LEAK): {decision:'INTERVENE', confidence:0.9, evidenceStrength:0.75, matchScore:0.75}; event confidence label '75%'; sessionSummary().lastAnalysis.confidence 0.9. Script C1 (privilege-only, no seed match): {decision:'INTERVENE', confidence:undefined, evidenceStrength:0.9, matchScore:undefined, stored:'X-CFS-004'}; stored fingerprint.confidence '—'; event label '—'; lastAnalysis.confidence undefined. Code: gateway.mjs:167 `confidence: Math.max(0.9, match.score)`; :52 `result.evidenceStrength = 0.9`; :54 `= 0.7`.
```

</details>

### F-044 · [medium] Default environment 'enterprise' fabricates cross-context claims when the client omits environment; defaults differ across modules

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/ops-agent.mjs`, `engine/sermg.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/gateway.mjs:152-154; engine/memory.mjs:241 (storeFingerprint default 'dev'); engine/ops-agent.mjs:102 (deliver default 'dev'); engine/sermg.mjs:55-57 (default 'cloud')

analyze() silently maps a missing or invalid environment to 'enterprise' and passes it to matchByInvariants as appliedIn. Because the seeds were 'learned' in dev/cloud, any request without an environment that matches a seed is reported with crossContext:true and the reason 'اكتُشف في التطوير، ومُنع في المؤسسة' — a cross-environment immunity claim the client never made. The same request's delivery event (ops-agent deliver) records environment 'dev', a fingerprint stored via POST /api/fingerprints without environment gets 'dev', and SERMG defaults to 'cloud', so one request can be tagged with two different environments and the 'بيئات اكتسبت المناعة' metric is inflated by a default. IncidentInputSchema declares environment optional, so omission is a legitimate input.

**Proposed fix:** Export one DEFAULT_ENVIRONMENT constant and use it in gateway, memory, ops-agent and sermg. When the request omits environment, do not assert cross-context: pass `environment: undefined` to matchByInvariants so appliedIn = learnedIn and crossContext = false (and say so in the reason), or reject invalid non-empty values with 400 via the schema. Add a test that a request without environment never yields crossContext:true.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Script section 3: analyze({...NOVEL_LEAK without environment}) => {decision:'INTERVENE', matchedSignature:'X-CFS-001', crossContext:true, learnedIn:'dev', appliedIn:'enterprise'}, reason 'اكتُشف في التطوير، ومُنع في المؤسسة. البصمة X-CFS-001.'; storeFingerprint({...no environment}) => stored env 'dev'. HTTP keyword probe without environment => crossContext:true, learnedIn:'cloud', appliedIn:'enterprise'. Code: gateway.mjs:152 `const environment = [...].includes(body.environment) ? body.environment : "enterprise"`.
```

</details>

### F-045 · [medium] Missing trustedDestination handled asymmetrically: triggers 'صلاحية مرتفعة' INTERVENE but never 'وجهة غير معتمدة', producing a one-node fingerprint

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/xcfs.mjs`, `engine/gateway.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/xcfs.mjs:53 vs :57-59; engine/gateway.mjs:211-229

deriveInvariants adds the destination invariant only when `trustedDestination === false`, but adds the privilege invariant when `elevatedPrivilege && !body.trustedDestination`, i.e. also when the field is undefined. A body with elevatedPrivilege:true and no trustedDestination therefore yields invariants ['صلاحية مرتفعة'] alone, fallback() returns INTERVENE, nothing matches (Jaccard 0.33 vs X-CFS-002), and analyze() auto-stores a fingerprint whose only node is 'صلاحية' with the destination node absent from keptNodes. The same input with trustedDestination:false stores a 3-invariant fingerprint. Two different memories for one causal shape, and the stored one-invariant fingerprint will then match (Jaccard 1.0) any later privilege-only request at confidence 1.0.

**Proposed fix:** Normalize once: `const untrusted = body.trustedDestination === false` and use it for both the destination and privilege conditions (privilege invariant = `elevatedPrivilege === true && untrusted`); with schema validation (finding 1) a missing boolean becomes a 400 instead of an implicit value. Add a unit test for the undefined case.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Script section 2: deriveInvariants({tool:'read_file', elevatedPrivilege:true, sensitiveData:false}) => ['صلاحية مرتفعة']; analyze => {decision:'INTERVENE', matchedSignature:'X-CFS-004', stored:'X-CFS-004', invariants:['صلاحية مرتفعة'], nodes:1}; destination node => undefined. Code: xcfs.mjs:53 `if (body.trustedDestination === false)`; :57 `if (body.elevatedPrivilege && !body.trustedDestination)`.
```

</details>

### F-046 · [medium] OpenAI call has no timeout/AbortController; a hung upstream hangs /api/analyze indefinitely (default UI path when OPENAI_API_KEY is set)

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/gateway.mjs`, `server.js`
- **Location:** engine/gateway.mjs:62-131 callOpenAI(), 181-200

callOpenAI() uses fetch() with no `signal`, and server.js configures no request/handler timeout (Node default server.timeout = 0). The UI's analyzeIncident() sends preferRules:false by default, so on a deployment with OPENAI_API_KEY the main /api/analyze path depends on this call; if api.openai.com stalls, the request never resolves, the rules fallback in the catch block is never reached, and the socket is held open. Parsing also has two misleading branches: `result.output_text` is an SDK-only convenience that the REST JSON never contains (dead first branch), and `status: "incomplete"` (truncated output) is not checked, so a truncated JSON surfaces as the warning 'تعذر اتصال الذكاء الاصطناعي' (connection failure) rather than a model/limit error.

**Proposed fix:** Add `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(Number(process.env.OPENAI_TIMEOUT_MS) || 8000)` to the fetch and let the existing catch produce the rules fallback with a distinct warning for timeout vs HTTP error vs incomplete output; check `result.status === "completed"` before parsing; drop the output_text branch. Also set server.requestTimeout/headersTimeout explicitly in server.js.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Script B1 with globalThis.fetch mocked to a never-resolving promise: after 1.5 s `analyze settled? false | signal passed to fetch? none`. Code: gateway.mjs:103 `await fetch("https://api.openai.com/v1/responses", { method, headers, body })` — no signal; :126-128 `result.output_text || result.output?.flatMap(...)`. grep -n timeout server.js => no matches. B7: a payload with status:'incomplete' and truncated text falls to rules with warning 'تعذر اتصال الذكاء الاصطناعي؛ استُخدم محرك القواعد الآمن.'
```

</details>

### F-047 · [medium] AI-path INTERVENE auto-stores fingerprints without checking invariants: unbounded empty-invariant fingerprints, memory pollution that tags benign ALLOWs with matchScore 1.0, duplicate stores under concurrency

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/memory.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/gateway.mjs:181-189 (AI branch), 211-229 (auto-store block); engine/memory.mjs:192-193, 236-260

The auto-store block runs for any INTERVENE without a match, including model decisions on bodies whose structural reduction produced no (or only the send) invariant. With zero invariants matchByInvariants returns null immediately (`if (!invariants.length) return null`), so every such INTERVENE stores a new fingerprint with invariants: [] that can never match and never deduplicates — the per-org store grows without bound from a single repeated request. With only ['أداة إرسال'] the stored fingerprint later matches every benign send at Jaccard 1.0: the ALLOW result then carries matchedSignature X-CFS-00N and matchScore 1 next to confidence 0.2, and the fingerprint's matches counter increments. Because the store happens after `await callOpenAI`, two concurrent requests for the same novel shape both see no match and store X-CFS-004 and X-CFS-005.

**Proposed fix:** Only auto-store when the structural evidence justifies a memory: `risky && reduction.invariants.length >= 2` (or when fallback() itself is at least VERIFY); skip storing for model-only INTERVENEs and return the decision with storedFingerprint absent. Immediately before storing, re-run matchByInvariants(reduction.invariants, { record:false, orgId }) and reuse an existing id if found, to close the await race. Add tests for the empty-invariant and send-only cases.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Script B4 (fetch mocked to return INTERVENE; body with trustedDestination:true, sensitiveData:false, elevatedPrivilege:false): fingerprints before 3, after 3 identical calls 6; new entries {id:'X-CFS-004', invariants:[], tags:[], nodes:3, confidence:'—'} ×3. B5: after one AI INTERVENE on BENIGN_SEND, the next benign send => {decision:'ALLOW', confidence:0.2, matchScore:1, matchedSignature:'X-CFS-004', invariants:['أداة إرسال']}; stored fp {id:'X-CFS-004', invariants:['أداة إرسال'], matches:1}. B8: Promise.all of two analyze() calls on one sub-threshold shape => stored ids X-CFS-004 and X-CFS-005.
```

</details>

### F-048 · [medium] Model-generated matchedSignature is passed through on VERIFY/ALLOW and counted by sessionSummary as a real fingerprint match (matchRate 1.0 for a non-existent X-CFS-777)

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/telemetry.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/gateway.mjs:73, 96 (schema requires matchedSignature), 187 (`match?.id || ai.matchedSignature || "NO-MATCH"`); engine/telemetry.mjs:121

The OpenAI JSON schema forces the model to emit a matchedSignature string, and the AI branch returns it to the caller when memory had no match (`match?.id || ai.matchedSignature`). Only INTERVENE results are later overwritten by the auto-store id; VERIFY/ALLOW results keep the fabricated id. sessionSummary counts any risky event whose matchedSignature startsWith('X-CFS') as a memory match, so one hallucinated id turns the 'دقة المطابقة في الجلسة' metric to 1.0 with no fingerprint involved — a measured-result claim the product-truth rules forbid. matchScore is undefined in that case, so the UI shows a signature with no score.

**Proposed fix:** Never take matchedSignature from the model: remove it from the OpenAI schema (or ignore it) and compute `matchedSignature` from `match` only, using the same PARTIAL-MATCH/NO-MATCH helper as the rules branch. In sessionSummary, count a match only when the id exists in listFingerprints(orgId) or when the event carries a numeric matchScore.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Script C2 (fetch mocked to return VERIFY with matchedSignature 'X-CFS-777' on the PARTIAL fixture): result.matchedSignature 'X-CFS-777', exists in memory? false; sessionSummary('c2').matchRate 1; event.matchedSignature 'X-CFS-777', risky true. B2: VERIFY with model-supplied 'X-CFS-001' returned to caller with matchScore undefined. Code: telemetry.mjs:121 `risky.filter((event) => String(event.matchedSignature || "").startsWith("X-CFS"))`.
```

### F-049 · [medium] PARTIAL-MATCH is not score-backed and is inconsistent across branches; the UI's INTERVENE+NO-MATCH branch is unreachable

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/memory.mjs`, `components/causaseal/investigate-view.tsx`
- **Location:** engine/gateway.mjs:48 (fallback 'NO-MATCH'), 196 vs 206, 211-229; engine/memory.mjs:200-205; components/causaseal/investigate-view.tsx:175

In the rules branch matchedSignature is `match?.id || (risky ? "PARTIAL-MATCH" : "NO-MATCH")`: PARTIAL-MATCH means only 'some non-send invariant exists', never that a fingerprint was partially close, because matchByInvariants discards the best sub-threshold score and returns null, so the gateway cannot report closeness (matchScore is undefined on every PARTIAL-MATCH). The AI-fallback branch (line 196) uses `match?.id || "NO-MATCH"`, so the identical input is labelled PARTIAL-MATCH in rules mode and NO-MATCH when the AI call failed. fallback() itself always sets 'NO-MATCH' and analyze overwrites it. Because INTERVENE always ends with match.id or the auto-stored id, an INTERVENE can never carry 'NO-MATCH', so the SAIF demo code path `data.decision === "INTERVENE" && data.matchedSignature === "NO-MATCH"` → handleSaveFingerprint never runs. The three sentinels are undocumented in contracts.mjs.

**Proposed fix:** Have matchByInvariants return the best candidate even below threshold (`{ matched:false, id, score }`) and let the gateway emit PARTIAL-MATCH only when a real sub-threshold score exists (e.g. ≥ 0.3) with matchScore populated; compute the sentinel in one helper used by all three branches; document ALLOW/VERIFY/INTERVENE × NO-MATCH/PARTIAL-MATCH/X-CFS-* in contracts.mjs; delete or rewrite the unreachable UI branch.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Script B6 on the PARTIAL fixture: AI-fallback => {decision:'VERIFY', matchedSignature:'NO-MATCH', warning:'تعذر اتصال…'}; rulesOnly => {decision:'VERIFY', matchedSignature:'PARTIAL-MATCH'}; both matchScore undefined. Code: gateway.mjs:196 `matchedSignature: match?.id || "NO-MATCH"`, :206 `matchedSignature: match?.id || (risky ? "PARTIAL-MATCH" : "NO-MATCH")`, :227 `result.matchedSignature = stored.id` whenever INTERVENE && !match; memory.mjs:200 `if (invariantScore < MATCH_THRESHOLD) continue`.
```

</details>

### F-050 · [medium] POST body `null` makes every JSON endpoint hang without a response (parseJsonBody sentinel collision)

- **Lens:** gateway-correctness · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `server.js`
- **Location:** server.js:178-187 parseJsonBody(); callers at 243-251 (/api/analyze), 275-282, 284-295 (/api/gateway/intercept), 297-306, 308-315

parseJsonBody returns `null` after it has already sent a 400 for invalid JSON, and every caller treats `body === null` as 'response already sent' and returns. But the literal JSON body `null` parses to null too, so the handler returns without writing any response: the connection stays open until the client gives up. With no server timeout configured (Node default server.timeout = 0) this is a trivial way to hold sockets open on the public deployment. Out of the gateway lens but confirmed while probing analyze() inputs; also affects /api/gateway/intercept, /api/fingerprints POST, /api/agent/run and /api/sermg/run.

**Proposed fix:** Make parseJsonBody return a discriminated result (`{ ok: true, body } | { ok: false }`) or throw a handled error, and reject non-object bodies (`body === null || typeof body !== "object" || Array.isArray(body)`) with 400. Set server.requestTimeout/headersTimeout and a handler-level timeout in server.js.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
curl -m 3 -X POST http://127.0.0.1:4100/api/analyze -d 'null' => http_code=000 time=3.002s, curl exit 28 (timed out waiting for response); same for /api/gateway/intercept; a first probe without -m hung for over 120 s. Body '"str"' => 200 in 16 ms. Code: server.js:181-185 `try { return JSON.parse(raw) } catch { sendJson(res, 400, …); return null }`; :245 `if (body === null) return`.
```

</details>

### F-051 · [medium] Language toggle has no effect on 8 of 13 routes: five views never read the language and two pages are server components

- **Lens:** i18n-completeness · **Category:** i18n · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/impact-view.tsx`, `components/causaseal/investigate-view.tsx`, `components/causaseal/lab-view.tsx`, `components/causaseal/memory-view.tsx`, `components/causaseal/reports-view.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/investigate/page.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/team/page.tsx`, `components/causaseal/overview-view.tsx`, `components/causaseal/monitor-view.tsx`, `lib/chart-demo.ts`, `lib/chart-session.ts`, `lib/seed-data.ts`, `lib/compliance-client.ts`
- **Location:** ImpactView, InvestigateView, LabView, MemoryView, ReportsView (no useLanguage at all); InvestigatePage/TeamPage (server components, hardcoded Arabic PageHeading); OverviewView/MonitorView (only t("session"), t("nav.monitor"), filter keys)

The header on every page offers an English toggle, but only /architecture, /connectors, /connectors/http, /connectors/mcp and /connectors/sdk actually translate their body. Impact, Investigate, Lab, Memory, Reports and Team render 100% Arabic copy in EN mode, and Overview/Monitor translate the heading and filter chips only, so a judge who switches to English sees an English sidebar/header wrapped around Arabic pages (mixed-language UI). The data layer behind these views (chart labels, demo rows, seed fingerprints, compliance notes, DEMO_METRICS labels) is also Arabic-only with no en field, so even the charts' legends and axis labels stay Arabic. /team is additionally an orphan route (built as out/team.html, not in NAV_I18N/NAV_ITEMS/search), so its header title falls back to t("shell.protection").

**Proposed fix:** Pick one of two honest options and apply it consistently: (a) finish the EN copy using the existing pattern — add `const { language } = useLanguage(); const ar = language === "ar"` to the five views, move their copy into `{ar, en}` catalogs the way lib/architecture.ts and lib/connectors.ts already do, give chart-demo/chart-session/seed-data/compliance-client `labelAr/labelEn` fields, and convert investigate/page.tsx + team/page.tsx headings into client components; or (b) if EN is out of scope for this stage, scope the toggle (hide it or show an "Arabic-only page" badge on untranslated routes) so the UI does not promise a translation it does not have. Either way add /team to NAV_I18N or remove the orphan route.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
grep -L useLanguage components/causaseal/*.tsx → causal-path, impact-view, investigate-view, lab-view, memory-view, reports-view, stat. Arabic-bearing line counts (grep -cP '\p{Arabic}'): investigate-view 65, impact-view 63, reports-view 46, lab-view 43, memory-view 23 — 240 lines, zero `ar ?` branches in those files. overview-view.tsx has 41 Arabic lines and the only dictionary call is `{t("session")}` (line 101); monitor-view.tsx has 28 Arabic lines and uses t() only at lines 136,142,230,239,256. app/(dashboard)/investigate/page.tsx:10-13 and team/page.tsx:36-40 are server components with `title="تحليل حادث جديد"` / `title="فريق العمل"`. lib/chart-demo.ts 33, lib/chart-session.ts 23, lib/seed-data.ts 16, lib/compliance-client.ts 7 Arabic lines, none with an en twin. docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md:395 itself lists "Full EN page copy … not fully translated yet" as open.
```

</details>

### F-052 · [medium] Architecture page (the judge proof) renders Arabic inside EN mode via formatDecision() and HARNESS_DIFF

- **Lens:** i18n-completeness · **Category:** i18n · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/architecture-view.tsx`, `lib/decisions.ts`, `lib/nav.ts`
- **Location:** architecture-view.tsx:219 (<CardDescription>{HARNESS_DIFF}</CardDescription>), :337 (formatDecision(row.decision)); lib/decisions.ts:1-16; lib/nav.ts:67-68

ArchitectureView is otherwise fully bilingual, but two shared helpers are Arabic-only: DECISION_LABELS/formatDecision always returns `"${arabicLabel} · ${code}"`, and HARNESS_DIFF is a single Arabic sentence. In English mode the Flow card description is an Arabic paragraph and the Fingerprint column of the four-case harness table shows e.g. "منع قبل الأداة · INTERVENE". This is the page AGENTS.md calls the judge proof, so the mixed language is visible exactly where reviewers look.

**Proposed fix:** Make the helpers language-aware: `DECISION_LABELS: Record<string,{ar:string;en:string}>` and `formatDecision(code, language)` (en: "Blocked before tool · INTERVENE"), and `HARNESS_DIFF = { ar: …, en: … }`; pass `language` from the three bilingual callers (architecture-view, and later overview/impact).

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
lib/decisions.ts:13-16: `export function formatDecision(code) { const ar = decisionLabel(code); return ar === code ? code : `${ar} · ${code}` }` with DECISION_LABELS = { ALLOW: "سماح", VERIFY: "تحقق بشري", INTERVENE: "منع قبل الأداة", … }. architecture-view.tsx:337 `{formatDecision(row.decision)}` inside the EN-capable table; :219 `<CardDescription>{HARNESS_DIFF}</CardDescription>` where lib/nav.ts:67 `export const HARNESS_DIFF = "نركب فوق هارنس الوكيل. …"`. Contrast line 165 of the same file, which correctly uses the raw `decision` in the en branch.
```

</details>

### F-053 · [medium] Directional chevrons point the wrong way in both RTL and LTR: rtl:rotate-180 is applied to glyphs that are already RTL-correct

- **Lens:** i18n-completeness · **Category:** ui · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/causal-path.tsx`, `components/causaseal/connectors-view.tsx`, `components/causaseal/connector-page-shell.tsx`, `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md`
- **Location:** causal-path.tsx:40 (ChevronLeftIcon rtl:rotate-180), connectors-view.tsx:76 (ChevronLeftIcon rtl:rotate-180), connector-page-shell.tsx:16 (ChevronRightIcon rtl:rotate-180); recipe at docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md:199,237

Lucide ChevronLeft points left, which is already the 'forward' direction in RTL. Adding `rtl:rotate-180` flips it to point right under dir=rtl (the default Arabic UI), so the arrows between causal-path nodes point against the flow (nodes run right→left, chevrons point right), the hub-card hover affordance points backwards, and the connector back-link chevron (ChevronRight + rtl:rotate-180) points left, i.e. forward. In LTR/English the unrotated glyphs are wrong in the opposite way. Only ArchitectureView gets it right by choosing the icon from `direction`.

**Proposed fix:** Use LTR-first glyphs with the RTL flip: causal-path and connectors-view → `ChevronRightIcon className="… rtl:rotate-180"`; connector-page-shell back link → `ChevronLeftIcon className="… rtl:rotate-180"` (or pick the icon from `direction` as architecture-view does). Fix the recipe in docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md:237 so it does not get re-applied.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Compiled CSS out/_next/static/css/3804e6c1edd575e2.css contains `.rtl\:rotate-180:where(:dir(rtl),[dir=rtl],[dir=rtl] *){rotate:180deg}` and out/index.html starts `<html lang="ar" dir="rtl"`, so the rotation is active by default. node_modules/lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/chevron-right.mjs path is `m9 18 6-6-6-6` (apex x=15 → points right); chevron-left is its mirror (`m15 18-6-6 6-6`, apex x=9 → points left). causal-path.tsx:16 lays nodes out with `md:flex-row` (first node at the right in RTL) and :40 renders `<ChevronLeftIcon className="size-4 text-muted-foreground rtl:rotate-180" />` between them. architecture-view.tsx:97 does the correct mapping: `const Arrow = direction === "rtl" ? ArrowLeftIcon : ArrowRightIcon`.
```

</details>

### F-054 · [medium] Engine persists locale-formatted display strings (ar-SA dates, en-GB server-local times) instead of ISO timestamps

- **Lens:** i18n-completeness · **Category:** i18n · **Breaking fix:** yes
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/ops-agent.mjs`, `connectors/service.mjs`, `lib/api/client.ts`, `lib/seed-data.ts`, `components/causaseal/memory-view.tsx`, `components/causaseal/monitor-view.tsx`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:253, lib/api/client.ts:69 (toLocaleDateString("ar-SA")); engine/gateway.mjs:247, engine/ops-agent.mjs:91, connectors/service.mjs:105,333,362 (toLocaleTimeString("en-GB",{hour12:false})); rendered raw at memory-view.tsx:120 and monitor-view.tsx:275

Fingerprint `date` and event `time` are formatted at write time with a fixed locale and the server's timezone, then stored in causaseal-state.json and returned over /api and MCP. Consequences: (1) the stored format cannot be re-rendered per viewer language — EN users get Arabic-Indic digits with RLM marks; (2) seeded fingerprints use a different format ("18 سبتمبر 2026", Latin digits, long month) than engine-created ones ("١٦‏/١٠‏/٢٠٢٦"), so the Memory grid shows two date styles side by side after one 'Save as fingerprint'; (3) event `time` is wall-clock in the Hostinger server's TZ with no date, so the monitor table and reports 'الوقت' column are not the viewer's local time and sort/compare only by string.

**Proposed fix:** Store machine values: `createdAt: new Date().toISOString()` for fingerprints and events (keep `date`/`time` as derived fields for one release if MCP consumers depend on them). Format in the client with `new Intl.DateTimeFormat(language === "ar" ? "ar" : "en", { dateStyle: "medium", timeStyle: "medium" })` so digits, calendar and timezone follow the viewer. Give seed-data ISO dates too so seeded and live rows match.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
engine/memory.mjs:253 `date: input.date || new Date().toLocaleDateString("ar-SA")`; node -e 'new Date("2026-10-16T12:00:00Z").toLocaleDateString("ar-SA")' → "١٦‏/١٠‏/٢٠٢٦" (numberingSystem "arab"). lib/seed-data.ts:25 `date: "18 سبتمبر 2026"`. engine/gateway.mjs:247 `time: new Date().toLocaleTimeString("en-GB", { hour12: false })` (same in ops-agent.mjs:91, connectors/service.mjs:105,333,362). engine/contracts.mjs:64 `date: z.string()`, :74 `time: z.string()`. memory-view.tsx:120 `<time className=…>{fp.date}</time>` and monitor-view.tsx:275 `{event.time}` render the strings verbatim with no Intl formatting.
```

</details>

### F-055 · [medium] LanguageProvider reads/writes localStorage unguarded inside the render snapshot; a storage SecurityError blanks the whole app

- **Lens:** i18n-completeness · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/language-provider.tsx`, `lib/api/client.ts`
- **Location:** language-provider.tsx:24-28 readStoredLanguage (used as useSyncExternalStore getSnapshot), :65-69 setLanguage; lib/api/client.ts:39,59,72

`readStoredLanguage()` is the client snapshot for useSyncExternalStore, so it runs during every render of the root provider. Accessing `localStorage` throws a SecurityError when the page is embedded in a sandboxed iframe without allow-same-origin, when Chrome is set to block all site data, or when Firefox has dom.storage disabled — all realistic for a public demo that judges may open inside a portal iframe. An exception in getSnapshot propagates out of render and there is no error boundary, so the entire dashboard renders blank instead of defaulting to Arabic. `setLanguage` has the same unguarded setItem (QuotaExceeded/SecurityError aborts before the LANGUAGE_EVENT dispatch, so the toggle silently does nothing). next-themes, used right next to it, guards its storage access.

**Proposed fix:** Wrap storage in a tiny safe accessor: `function safeGet(){ try { return localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY) } catch { return null } }` and `safeSet` likewise; keep the in-memory language as the source of truth (a module-level variable updated by setLanguage) so the toggle still works when storage is unavailable. Apply the same guard to lib/api/client.ts fallbacks.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
language-provider.tsx:25-27 `if (typeof window === "undefined") return "ar"; const raw = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY); return raw === "en" ? "en" : "ar"` — no try/catch; :55-59 `React.useSyncExternalStore(subscribeLanguage, getClientLanguage, getServerLanguage)` where getClientLanguage = readStoredLanguage. :66 `localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, lang)` precedes `window.dispatchEvent(new Event(LANGUAGE_EVENT))`. grep localStorage lib components → the only guarded uses are none; client.ts:39 getItem sits inside a catch block, so a throw there escapes.
```

</details>

### F-056 · [medium] formatResult truncation emits invalid JSON text and leaves structuredContent untruncated

- **Lens:** mcp-protocol-conformance · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `connectors/service.mjs`
- **Location:** connectors/service.mjs:70-95 formatResult(), specifically `text = JSON.stringify(truncated, null, 2).slice(0, CHARACTER_LIMIT)` and `structured = truncated`

When a tool result exceeds CHARACTER_LIMIT (25000), formatResult spreads the full data into `truncated`, adds `truncated: true` and a message, then cuts the serialized string at 25000 characters. The `content[0].text` block is therefore not parseable JSON (the client gets a hard cut mid-document), while `structuredContent` still carries the entire oversized payload (nothing was actually truncated), so the limit protects nothing and the `truncation_message` advises `response_format=json` even when the caller already used json. Reachable with limit=50 pages of events/fingerprints with node arrays or `causaseal_run_sermg` count=24 in json mode.

**Proposed fix:** Truncate the data, not the string: when over the limit, shrink the page arrays (events/fingerprints/results/controls or any array-valued property) until the serialized size fits, set `truncated: true` plus `next_offset`, and serialize that object for both `text` and `structuredContent` so the text is always valid JSON. Add a unit test in test/connectors-service.test.mjs asserting JSON.parse(text) succeeds and structuredContent is within the limit.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe: `formatResult({rows: 3000 x {i,text}}, "json")` -> `text length: 25000 limit: 25000`; `truncated text parses as JSON: NO -> Unexpected end of JSON input`; `structuredContent.truncated: true structuredContent rows: 3000`. Code: service.mjs:81-90 `const truncated = { ...data, truncated: true, truncation_message: ... }; structured = truncated; text = JSON.stringify(truncated, null, 2).slice(0, CHARACTER_LIMIT)`.
```

</details>

### F-057 · [medium] server.js routes GET and DELETE /mcp into the stateless transport: GET opens a dangling SSE stream that can never deliver anything

- **Lens:** mcp-protocol-conformance · **Category:** mcp · **Breaking fix:** yes
- **Files:** `server.js`
- **Location:** server.js:207-230 `if (pathname === "/mcp") { ... await transport.handleRequest(req, res, parsedBody) }` (no method check)

The transport is created with `sessionIdGenerator: undefined` (stateless) and a brand-new McpServer per request. Any GET /mcp with Accept: text/event-stream is accepted with 200 text/event-stream and held open indefinitely, yet no code path will ever write to that stream (there is no session and the per-request server is discarded), so the connection is pure resource holding by unauthenticated clients. DELETE /mcp returns 200 OK although there is no session to delete. The SDK's documented stateless pattern only wires POST (README: `app.post('/mcp', ...)`); GET/DELETE should be refused with 405.

**Proposed fix:** In server.js, only hand POST to the transport; answer GET and DELETE on /mcp with 405 and a JSON-RPC error body (`{"jsonrpc":"2.0","error":{"code":-32000,"message":"Method not allowed."},"id":null}`) plus `Allow: POST, OPTIONS`. Add a test to test/connectors-mcp.test.mjs for GET -> 405.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
curl against server.js on :4111: `GET /mcp` -> `HTTP/1.1 200 OK ... content-type: text/event-stream ... Transfer-Encoding: chunked`, connection stayed open until `--max-time 5` expired (a first probe without a timeout hung for 60 s). `DELETE /mcp` -> `HTTP/1.1 200 OK content-type: text/plain; charset=UTF-8`. node_modules/@modelcontextprotocol/node/README.md stateless example registers only `app.post('/mcp', ...)`.
```

</details>

### F-058 · [medium] matchByInvariants with record:false still mutates coveredEnvironments and the change is flushed to disk by any later unrelated write

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/persist.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:209-221 (mutation at 209-214 happens before the `options.record !== false` check at 215)

The coveredEnvironments mutation is performed unconditionally and only the `matches` increment and saveFingerprints are gated by `record`. Because loadFingerprints returns the live objects from the persist cache, a read-only probe (record:false) changes the cached org state; the next writeState from any module (e.g. telemetry.recordEvent for a different org) serialises the whole cache and persists the probe's side effect. The in-memory view and the disk file disagree in between, and "record: false" does not mean what callers (tests use it as a pure query) assume.

**Proposed fix:** Move the coveredEnvironments update inside `if (options.record !== false)` and compute it on a copied object (see the seed-mutation fix); return `appliedIn`/`crossContext` from local variables without touching stored state when record is false.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe: persisted orgP, then `matchByInvariants(["صلاحية مرتفعة","أداة إرسال","وجهة غير معتمدة"], { orgId:"orgP", environment:"cloud", record:false })`. Disk X-CFS-002.coveredEnvironments right after: ['enterprise']; in-memory listFingerprints("orgP"): ['enterprise','cloud']. After `recordEvent({...}, { orgId: "someOtherOrg" })` disk X-CFS-002.coveredEnvironments: ['enterprise','cloud'].
```

</details>

### F-059 · [medium] immunizedEnvironments counts illustrative seed environments, so a fresh org reports 3/3 'environments immunized'

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `server.js`, `connectors/service.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:178-186 immunizedEnvironments; server.js:347-350 and connectors/service.mjs:209-212 metric "بيئات اكتسبت المناعة" = Math.min(immunizedEnvironments(orgId).length, 3) / 3

The three seeds carry coveredEnvironments dev, enterprise and cloud. immunizedEnvironments unions them regardless of origin, so any org returns all three environments before a single decision has been made. As soon as sessionSummary.hasSession is true (one benign ALLOW is enough) /api/reports/metrics and the MCP causaseal_get_report show the immunity metric at 1.0 (100%). This contradicts the product rule to claim only what the prototype proves, and fingerprintsAreIllustrative(orgId) (true for that org) is not consulted.

**Proposed fix:** Count only environments that were actually learned or applied in this org: iterate fingerprints with origin === "stored" or with matches > 0, or derive from the org's session events (sessionSummary already has `environments`). Alternatively return [] when fingerprintsAreIllustrative(orgId) and label the metric illustrative in the report payload.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe on a fresh scratch state: `immunizedEnvironments("orgB")` -> ['dev','enterprise','cloud'] while `listFingerprints("orgB")` are all origin 'seed' and `fingerprintsAreIllustrative("orgB")` -> true. server.js:349 / service.mjs:211 compute `Math.min(3,3)/3 = 1`.
```

</details>

### F-060 · [medium] crossContext/learnedIn narrate learning history for seed matches; default environment makes every demo analyze claim 'discovered in dev, blocked in enterprise'

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** yes
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/gateway.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:207-208, 225-227 (`learnedIn = best.fingerprint.environment || "dev"`, `crossContext: learnedIn !== appliedIn`); engine/gateway.mjs:152-154 (environment defaults to "enterprise"), 168-170 (reason string)

learnedIn is taken from the fingerprint's `environment` even when the fingerprint is an illustrative seed that was never learned. The gateway coerces a missing/invalid environment to "enterprise", and seed X-CFS-001 is "dev", so any leak-shaped request on a fresh org (the default state for every new orgId and for the live demo after a state reset) returns crossContext:true with the reason "اكتُشف في التطوير، ومُنع في المؤسسة. البصمة X-CFS-001" although nothing was discovered in dev. The result object carries no indication that the matched fingerprint is a seed, so UI and MCP replies present fabricated learning history.

**Proposed fix:** Return the matched fingerprint's `origin` from matchByInvariants and, when origin === "seed", set `learnedIn: null`, `crossContext: false` (or `illustrativeMatch: true`) and use a reason such as "طابق بصمة توضيحية X-CFS-001"; only report cross-context when the matched fingerprint was stored in this org. Add `origin`/`illustrativeMatch` to AnalysisResultSchema so the UI can badge it.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe: `analyze({...NOVEL_LEAK, environment:"enterprise"}, { rulesOnly:true, orgId:"orgFreshCross" })` -> { matched:'X-CFS-001', learnedIn:'dev', appliedIn:'enterprise', crossContext:true, reason:'اكتُشف في التطوير، ومُنع في المؤسسة. البصمة X-CFS-001.' } while `fingerprintsAreIllustrative("orgFreshCross")` -> true. With `environment: undefined` -> appliedIn 'enterprise', crossContext true, same reason.
```

</details>

### F-061 · [medium] No dedupe on store: identical invariant sets create duplicate fingerprints and the strict '>' selection starves all but the first

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `server.js`, `connectors/mcp-server.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:236-259 storeFingerprint (no lookup before push at 257); engine/memory.mjs:201 (`if (!best || invariantScore > best.score)`)

storeFingerprint always appends. POST /api/fingerprints and the MCP causaseal_store_fingerprint tool therefore create any number of fingerprints with the same invariant set (score 1.0 against each other). matchByInvariants keeps the first fingerprint that reaches the best score (strict `>`), so the newer duplicates never receive matches or coveredEnvironments updates, the memory page shows misleading per-fingerprint counters, and structureScore is not used as a tie-breaker even though it is computed for every candidate.

**Proposed fix:** In storeFingerprint, look up an existing fingerprint in the org with the same invariant set (compare sorted unique arrays, or jaccard === 1) and return it (optionally merging environment into coveredEnvironments and updating desc) instead of appending; expose `created: false` to callers. In matchByInvariants break ties by structureScore, then by most recent/most matched.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe: three stores with invariants ['dup-a','dup-b','أداة إرسال'] in orgDup -> ids X-CFS-004, X-CFS-005, X-CFS-006 all accepted. `matchByInvariants(sameInvariants, { orgId:"orgDup", nodes:[{label:"مصدر الإدخال"},{label:"بيانات"}] })` -> { id:'X-CFS-004', score:1, graphScore:0 } although X-CFS-005 has those exact node labels (graphScore would be 1). Counters afterwards: X-CFS-004 matches 1, X-CFS-005 0, X-CFS-006 0.
```

</details>

### F-062 · [medium] graphScore is computed for every candidate but never affects selection or the decision, and seed node shapes invert it

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/contracts.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:199 (structureScore computed before the threshold check), 201 (ignored in selection), 228 (only echoed); engine/memory.mjs:15-20 seed X-CFS-001 nodes; engine/gateway.mjs:179; engine/contracts.mjs:53

matchByInvariants runs graphSimilarity against every fingerprint (including ones below threshold, which are then skipped) and the result is only copied into the return value as graphScore; it influences neither which fingerprint wins nor ALLOW/VERIFY/INTERVENE. The value is surfaced in AnalysisResultSchema and MCP replies as if it were part of the match. In addition seed X-CFS-001 lists invariant "أداة إرسال" but its nodes omit the "استدعاء أداة" node that xcfs.reduce marks critical for a send tool, so the exact-shape leak (Jaccard 1.0) gets graphScore 0.8 while the mutated leak without a send tool (Jaccard 0.75) gets graphScore 1.0. A reviewer reading this sees a decorative metric that is also wrong.

**Proposed fix:** Either make the structure score part of the operating point (e.g. tie-break by structureScore, or require structureScore >= 0.5 alongside Jaccard, with a test) or drop graphScore from the result/contract. Compute graphSimilarity only after the Jaccard threshold passes. Fix seed X-CFS-001 nodes to include {label:"استدعاء أداة", value:"send_to_workspace", risk:true} so seed shapes match what xcfs.reduce produces.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe with xcfs.reduce + memory.graphSimilarity against seed X-CFS-001: NOVEL_LEAK jaccard 1.00 graphScore 0.80 (kept labels مصدر الإدخال|تأثير القرار|استدعاء أداة|بيانات|سياق الوجهة); MUTATED_LEAK jaccard 0.75 graphScore 1.00 (labels مصدر الإدخال|تأثير القرار|بيانات|سياق الوجهة); seed labels مصدر الإدخال|تأثير القرار|بيانات|سياق الوجهة. Code: `if (invariantScore < MATCH_THRESHOLD) continue; if (!best || invariantScore > best.score) best = {...}` never reads structureScore. grep over docs/, lib/architecture.ts, lib/i18n.ts finds no mention of graphScore.
```

</details>

### F-063 · [medium] Fingerprint ids are derived from list length; two processes on one state file collide and silently drop writes

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/persist.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:239 (`X-CFS-${String(fingerprints.length + 1).padStart(3, "0")}`); engine/memory.mjs:217-220 (saveFingerprints map by id); engine/persist.mjs:27-47 (module-level cache, whole-file overwrite)

The id is the current in-memory list length plus one, and persist.mjs caches the whole state per process and rewrites the entire file from that cache. Any second process using the same data/causaseal-state.json (a second `node server.js`, `npm run dev` API plus a manual server, a stuck old instance during a Hostinger restart, or an MCP stdio launch) works from a stale cache: it mints the same X-CFS-00N id and its write overwrites the other process's fingerprint. Because matchByInvariants replaces rows by id (`item.id === best.fingerprint.id`), colliding ids inside one list would also both be overwritten by the winner.

**Proposed fix:** Mint ids from a persisted per-org counter or from `max(numeric suffix of existing ids) + 1` read fresh from disk at store time (or use a short random/UUID suffix), and in writeState re-read the file (or compare mtime/version) before writing so a stale cache cannot clobber newer state; log a warning on conflict.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe: process A storeFingerprint -> X-CFS-004 (written). Child process (same CAUSASEAL_STATE_PATH) storeFingerprint -> "B stored X-CFS-005 list len in B: 5" (written). Process A storeFingerprint again -> X-CFS-005 (collision). File afterwards: ['X-CFS-001..003 seeds', 'X-CFS-004:proc-A first', 'X-CFS-005:proc-A second'] — proc-B's fingerprint is gone.
```

</details>

### F-064 · [medium] persist.writeState is not atomic and a torn/corrupt state file is silently reset, wiping every org on the next write

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/persist.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/persist.mjs:27-35 readState (catch -> cache = {}); engine/persist.mjs:40-47 writeState (direct fs.writeFileSync)

writeState serialises the whole state and writes it in place with writeFileSync; a crash, OOM kill or disk-full mid-write leaves a truncated JSON file. readState swallows the parse error and treats the state as `{}` (so all orgs fall back to seeds), and the very next writeState overwrites the damaged file with only the new org, permanently deleting every other org's fingerprints and session events with no log line or backup. For a product whose pitch is 'remember why a past path was dangerous', silent memory loss is a core-path defect.

**Proposed fix:** Write to `${file}.tmp` then fs.renameSync over the target (atomic on POSIX); on JSON.parse failure rename the corrupt file to `${file}.corrupt-<timestamp>`, console.error it, and start from `{}` only after preserving the backup. Consider a `version`/`updatedAt` field in the file.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe: stored fingerprints in org1 and org2 (orgs on disk: ['demo','org1','org2']); truncated the file to half its length to simulate a torn write; resetPersistCache() (restart): `listFingerprints("org1")` -> seeds only ('LOST -> seeds'); one storeFingerprint in org3 -> orgs on disk: ['demo','org1','org3'] (org2 gone, org1 reduced to seeds).
```

</details>

### F-065 · [medium] Fingerprint memory grows without bound through unauthenticated stores; every store rewrites the whole file and GET /api/fingerprints returns everything

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** perf · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `server.js`, `engine/persist.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:257-258 (push + saveFingerprints), engine/persist.mjs:45 (full-file JSON.stringify per write); server.js:266-273 GET /api/fingerprints (no pagination), server.js:275-281 POST (no limit)

There is no cap, eviction or dedupe for stored fingerprints, and each store (or each matchByInvariants with record) serialises and writes the entire state file synchronously on the event loop, so cost per write is O(total state). /api/fingerprints and /mcp are public with caller-chosen orgId, so a loop of POSTs makes the state file and the per-request latency grow unboundedly, blocks the single-threaded server during each write, and GET /api/fingerprints (used by the memory page) returns the full array. Auto-stored fingerprints from analyze are bounded by the 5-label invariant vocabulary, so the exposure is the manual store path.

**Proposed fix:** Cap stored fingerprints per org (e.g. MAX_FINGERPRINTS = 500, evict oldest origin:'stored' with matches 0), dedupe identical invariant sets, and in server.js paginate GET /api/fingerprints with the existing paginate() helper (limit/offset query params, defaults that the UI already handles). Optionally debounce/coalesce writeState.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe: 1500 storeFingerprint calls in one org took 4641 ms and produced a 1,000,869-byte state file; the next 500 stores took 3246 ms (6.5 ms each vs 3.1 ms, i.e. growing with file size). matchByInvariants over 2003 fingerprints: 10 ms. Code: storeFingerprint has no length check; server.js GET handler returns `listFingerprints(orgId)` with no slice while connectors/service.mjs already has paginate().
```

</details>

### F-066 · [medium] Memory tests are vacuous: 'cross-context after learning' passes without the learning step and org isolation test misses the seed-mutation leak

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** test · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `test/gateway.test.mjs`
- **Location:** test/gateway.test.mjs:79-88 ("cross-context blocks after learning in dev"); test/gateway.test.mjs:173-207 ("org alpha fingerprints are invisible to org beta")

The cross-context test runs a leak in dev and then the same shape in enterprise and asserts crossContext === true. Both runs match seed X-CFS-001 (environment "dev") with Jaccard 1.0, nothing is stored (storedFingerprint undefined), and the second call alone on a fresh org already yields crossContext true, so the test proves only that the seed's environment differs from "enterprise", not that memory learned anything. The org-isolation test only checks that a unique stored invariant is invisible to another org; it does not assert that seeds, coveredEnvironments or matches of org beta are untouched after activity in org alpha, so the shared-SEEDS mutation ships green. There are no tests for storeFingerprint input validation, duplicate stores, or id generation.

**Proposed fix:** Rewrite the cross-context test to use invariants that no seed covers (so the first run stores a fingerprint; assert `first.result.storedFingerprint`), then assert the second run's matchedSignature equals that stored id and learnedIn === 'dev'. Extend the isolation test to snapshot `listFingerprints("beta")` before and after a match in alpha with a new environment and assert deep equality. Add tests: storeFingerprint rejects non-array nodes/tags/invariants and invalid environment; storing identical invariants twice returns the same id; ids are unique after reload.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe: `runOpsAgent({ kind:"cross", orgId:"orgCrossOnly", environment:"enterprise" })` with no prior run -> { decision:'INTERVENE', crossContext:true, matched:'X-CFS-001', stored:undefined }. Probe 1 showed org C's seed coveredEnvironments changed by org A's match while `npm test` baseline is 27/27 green.
```

</details>

### F-067 · [medium] LanguageProvider reads localStorage unguarded in a render-time snapshot; blocked storage crashes the whole app with no error boundary

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/language-provider.tsx`, `app/layout.tsx`
- **Location:** components/causaseal/language-provider.tsx:24-28 readStoredLanguage (used as useSyncExternalStore getSnapshot at :55-59) and :65-69 setLanguage

getSnapshot runs on every render of LanguageProvider (the root of the whole tree). It calls localStorage.getItem with no try/catch. In Chrome with cookies/site data blocked, in Safari/Chrome when the site is embedded in a third-party iframe (e.g. a submission portal preview), and in some private modes, accessing window.localStorage throws a DOMException SecurityError. The throw happens during render, there is no app/error.tsx or app/global-error.tsx, so the static export renders a blank page. setLanguage has the same unguarded localStorage.setItem. By contrast next-themes (same tree) wraps every localStorage access in try/catch.

**Proposed fix:** Wrap both reads and the write in try/catch (return "ar" on failure; keep an in-memory fallback so the toggle still works for the session). Add app/error.tsx and app/global-error.tsx client components so a render error shows a recoverable message instead of a blank page.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
language-provider.tsx:24-28: `function readStoredLanguage(): Language { if (typeof window === "undefined") return "ar"; const raw = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY); return raw === "en" ? "en" : "ar" }` and :66 `localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, lang)`. `find app -name error.tsx -o -name global-error.tsx` returns nothing. node_modules/next-themes/dist/index.mjs: `try{s=localStorage.getItem(e)||void 0}catch(u){}` and `try{localStorage.setItem(m,r)}catch(v){}` — the vendored provider guards, the project provider does not.
```

</details>

### F-068 · [medium] Overview live feed uses time-agent-tool as React key; every harness compare produces four identical keys

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/overview-view.tsx`, `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/ops-agent.mjs`
- **Location:** components/causaseal/overview-view.tsx:223 `key={`${event.time}-${event.agent}-${event.tool}`}`

Events carry a second-resolution `time` (toLocaleTimeString en-GB) and the demo agent is always 'Operations Assistant' / 'send_to_workspace'. One POST /api/agent/run records an intercept event and a deliver event in the same second with the same agent and tool, so `session.recent` (last 4) yields duplicate keys. React logs 'Encountered two children with the same key' and may reuse the wrong DOM row when the 4-second poll replaces the list, so the badge/label of one row can show on another.

**Proposed fix:** Give every recorded event a stable id in the engine (e.g. `id: crypto.randomUUID()` in gateway.mjs and ops-agent.mjs before recordEvent, add `id: z.string().optional()` to AgentEventSchema) and key on `event.id`. Until then, key on `${event.time}-${event.label}-${event.source}-${index}` as monitor-view already does with index.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Ran server.js on :4132 with a scratch state file, POSTed kinds mutated/leak/lookalike/partial to /api/agent/run, then GET /api/session and computed the component's key for each `recent` item: all four were `00:25:42-Operations Assistant-send_to_workspace` (3 duplicates). engine/gateway.mjs:247 and engine/ops-agent.mjs:91 both set `time: new Date().toLocaleTimeString("en-GB", { hour12: false })` and no event id is assigned.
```

</details>

### F-069 · [medium] Reports decisions table keys collide (time-decision) on every harness compare

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/reports-view.tsx`, `engine/telemetry.mjs`
- **Location:** components/causaseal/reports-view.tsx:288 `key={`${row.time}-${row.decision}`}`

`decisions` is `events.slice(0, 8)` mapped to {decision: label, note: path, time}. Two INTERVENE rows and two DELIVERED-REDACTED rows land in the same second during the four-case compare, producing duplicate keys and React key warnings on a page that polls every 4 seconds.

**Proposed fix:** Carry the event id (see overview finding) into the decisions projection in telemetry.mjs and key on it; or key on `${row.time}-${row.decision}-${index}` in the view.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
After the four /api/agent/run calls, GET /api/reports/metrics returned decisions whose computed keys were `00:25:42-VERIFY, 00:25:42-DELIVERED, 00:25:42-ALLOW, 00:25:42-DELIVERED-REDACTED, 00:25:42-INTERVENE, 00:25:42-DELIVERED-REDACTED, 00:25:42-INTERVENE` — duplicates: `00:25:42-DELIVERED-REDACTED`, `00:25:42-INTERVENE`. engine/telemetry.mjs:174-178 builds decisions without any id.
```

</details>

### F-070 · [medium] Monitor polling effect sets state without a cancellation guard: stale responses overwrite the current filter's results

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/monitor-view.tsx`
- **Location:** components/causaseal/monitor-view.tsx:120-127 (interval effect), :59-62 reload(), :100-118 (initial effect)

The second effect polls every 4s with `.then((data) => setEvents(data.events))` and no `cancelled` flag. When `filter` or `query` changes, the interval is cleared, but a request already in flight from the old filter resolves after the new filter's fetch and replaces `events` with the old filter's rows, so the table shows e.g. 'blocked' rows under the 'allowed' filter until the next tick. `reload()` (used after intercept) has the same unguarded setState. Separately, `query` is in both dependency arrays, so every keystroke fires a request and tears down/recreates the interval with no debounce.

**Proposed fix:** Merge the two effects into one: declare `let cancelled = false` and an AbortController, run the fetch immediately and on the interval through one `load()` that checks `cancelled` before setState, abort/clear in cleanup. Debounce `query` (e.g. 250ms via a `deferredQuery` with useDeferredValue or a small debounce hook). Drop `reload()` in favour of the same guarded `load`.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
monitor-view.tsx:120-127: `React.useEffect(() => { const timer = window.setInterval(() => { void fetchEvents({ status: filter, q: query }).then((data) => setEvents(data.events)).catch(() => {}) }, 4000); return () => window.clearInterval(timer) }, [filter, query])` — contrast with the first effect at :100-118 which does guard with `if (!cancelled)`. :59-62 `async function reload() { const data = await fetchEvents({ status: filter, q: query }); setEvents(data.events) }`.
```

</details>

### F-071 · [medium] Sidebar X-CFS memory badge is a hard-coded 3 that never reflects stored fingerprints

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/app-sidebar.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/layout.tsx`
- **Location:** components/causaseal/app-sidebar.tsx:50 `AppSidebar({ memoryCount = 3 })` and :91 `<SidebarMenuBadge>{memoryCount}</SidebarMenuBadge>`; app/(dashboard)/layout.tsx:18 `<AppSidebar />`

The only render site passes no prop, so the badge always says 3. After the investigate page saves a fingerprint (or MCP causaseal_store_fingerprint runs) the memory page lists 4+ fingerprints while the nav badge still says 3. This is a planted number in the shell, which conflicts with the project rule to show only session-proven numbers.

**Proposed fix:** Either fetch `fetchSession()` in AppSidebar (guarded effect, or a small shared session hook/context used by overview and connectors too) and render `session.fingerprintCount`, or remove the badge and the `badgeKey` from lib/nav.ts.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
`grep -rn memoryCount` finds only app-sidebar.tsx:47,50,91 — no caller passes it. POST /api/fingerprints on the test server created X-CFS-004 and GET /api/fingerprints returned 4 fingerprints; the sidebar would still render 3. /api/session already exposes `fingerprintCount` (lib/api/client.ts:112).
```

</details>

### F-072 · [medium] Memory page always shows the Illustrative badge and discards the API's illustrative flag

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/memory-view.tsx`
- **Location:** components/causaseal/memory-view.tsx:66 `<IllustrativeBadge />` (unconditional); :46-49 `.then((data) => { if (!cancelled) setFingerprints(data.fingerprints) })`

Every other view toggles the Illustrative badge against live data (overview uses hasSession, reports uses fromSession, lab/impact use result length). Memory renders the badge unconditionally and throws away `data.illustrative`, so after a judge stores a fingerprint from the investigate page the memory page still labels the real stored fingerprints as illustrative data.

**Proposed fix:** Add `const [illustrative, setIllustrative] = useState(true)`, set it from `data.illustrative` in the same then-branch, and render `{illustrative ? <IllustrativeBadge /> : <Badge variant="success">هذه الجلسة</Badge>}` as the other views do.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
GET /api/fingerprints on the test server returned `illustrative: true` with the 3 seeds; after POST /api/fingerprints it returned `illustrative: false` with 4 items (server.js:270 `illustrative: fingerprintsAreIllustrative(orgId)`, engine/memory.mjs:162-165). memory-view.tsx never reads that field.
```

</details>

### F-073 · [medium] Client-side compliance fallback claims 'mapped' controls the engine never proved and diverges from engine/compliance.mjs

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `lib/compliance-client.ts`, `components/causaseal/reports-view.tsx`
- **Location:** lib/compliance-client.ts:5-43 evaluate(); components/causaseal/reports-view.tsx:65 initial state and :113-115 catch fallback

The header comment says the file 'mirrors engine/compliance.mjs' but it is a stale copy: it hard-codes NCA-2 and OWASP-ASI06 as 'mapped' and NCA-1's note says persistent storage is not enabled yet, while the engine computes every status from session evidence (all 'partial'/'planned' with an empty session) and does persist to a JSON file. The reports page renders this copy on first paint and permanently whenever /api/compliance fails, so a judge can see compliance claims that the server would not make — contrary to 'claim only what the prototype proves'.

**Proposed fix:** Delete lib/compliance-client.ts. Initialise `controls` to `[]`, show a 'no server evidence' empty row/skeleton until /api/compliance answers, and on error show an explicit unreachable message instead of fabricated statuses. If an offline default is wanted, derive it from the engine module's zero-evidence branch (all partial/planned) rather than a hand-maintained copy.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
lib/compliance-client.ts:18 `status: "mapped"` (NCA-2), :39 `status: "mapped"` (OWASP-ASI06), :12 notes `التخزين الدائم غير مفعّل بعد`. engine/compliance.mjs: `status: sensitiveBlock ? "mapped" : "partial"`, `status: memoryPoison ? "mapped" : "planned"`, NCA-1 note `التخزين على القرص جاهز…`. reports-view.tsx:65 `useState<ComplianceControl[]>(() => evaluateCompliance())` and :114 `.catch(() => { if (!cancelled) setControls(evaluateCompliance()) })`.
```

</details>

### F-074 · [medium] connectors-http-view: set-state-in-effect lint error; effect calls un-cancellable refresh() and omits it from deps

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/connectors-http-view.tsx`
- **Location:** components/causaseal/connectors-http-view.tsx:54-64 refresh() and the mount effect

`npm run lint` fails on this line (react-hooks/set-state-in-effect, error level in eslint-config-next 16). The effect calls a component-scoped async function that does `setSession(await fetchSession())` with no cancellation, so navigating away during the request still calls setState on the unmounted view, and `refresh` is not in the dependency array. The sibling views (connectors-sdk-view.tsx:32-42, connectors-view.tsx:35-45) already use the correct pattern.

**Proposed fix:** Replace the effect body with the guarded form used elsewhere: `let cancelled = false; void fetchSession().then((d) => { if (!cancelled) setSession(d) }).catch(() => {}); return () => { cancelled = true }`. Keep `refresh()` only for the post-run refresh inside runMutated (or share one `useSession()` hook across the four connector views to remove the duplication entirely).

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
`npx eslint components/causaseal` → `connectors-http-view.tsx:63:10 error … Avoid calling setState() directly within an effect react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`. Code: `async function refresh() { try { setSession(await fetchSession()) } catch { /* ignore */ } }` … `React.useEffect(() => { void refresh() }, [])`. eslint --print-config shows set-state-in-effect at level 2 (error).
```

</details>

### F-075 · [medium] connectors-mcp-view: derived device URL stored in state and set synchronously in an effect (lint error)

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/connectors-mcp-view.tsx`, `lib/connectors.ts`
- **Location:** components/causaseal/connectors-mcp-view.tsx:36 `useState(LOCAL_MCP_URL)` and :38-39 `React.useEffect(() => { setDeviceUrl(resolveLocalMcpUrl()) …`

`deviceUrl` is a pure function of window.location (and NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE); it never changes after mount, so it is derived state. Setting it synchronously inside the effect is the second lint error that fails `npm run lint` and causes an extra render on every mount. A lazy `useState(() => resolveLocalMcpUrl())` would be wrong under static export (hydration mismatch: the exported HTML contains the server value). The project already has the correct idiom in theme-toggle.tsx (useIsClient via useSyncExternalStore).

**Proposed fix:** Delete the state and the setState line; use `const deviceUrl = React.useSyncExternalStore(() => () => {}, resolveLocalMcpUrl, () => LOCAL_MCP_URL)` (getSnapshot returns a primitive string, so no tearing/loop). Keep the fetchSession part of the effect unchanged.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
`npx eslint components/causaseal` → `connectors-mcp-view.tsx:39:5 error … Calling setState synchronously within an effect can trigger cascading renders react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`. `grep -c "127.0.0.1:4000/mcp" out/connectors/mcp.html` → 1, confirming the server snapshot is baked into the export. lib/connectors.ts:125-132 resolveLocalMcpUrl returns a stable string.
```

</details>

### F-076 · [medium] beforeTool has no timeout and accepts no AbortSignal, so a hung gateway blocks the partner's send tool indefinitely

- **Lens:** sdk-client · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `connectors/sdk.mjs`
- **Location:** connectors/sdk.mjs:28-34 (fetch call), 13 (options JSDoc)
