### F-125 · [low] Hand-wired node:http /mcp has no Host/Origin guard despite the SDK README instruction and the transport spec's Origin MUST

- **Lens:** mcp-protocol-conformance · **Category:** security · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `server.js`
- **Location:** server.js:207-230 /mcp branch (no validateHost/validateOrigin before transport.handleRequest)

The installed @modelcontextprotocol/node README states that with plain node:http you must compose the Host/Origin guards yourself (`hostHeaderValidation`/`originValidation`) because there is no middleware chain, and the Streamable HTTP spec requires servers to validate Origin. server.js passes every request straight to the transport. For a public remote server the DNS-rebinding risk is lower than for localhost, but the guard is a two-line addition and its absence is visible to any reviewer comparing against the SDK docs.

**Proposed fix:** Use `originValidation(["causaseal.aboalrejal.com", "localhost", "127.0.0.1"])` (requests without Origin still pass, so non-browser MCP clients are unaffected) and optionally `hostHeaderValidation` with the production hostname + localhost, invoked before the /mcp transport call.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
node_modules/@modelcontextprotocol/node/README.md: "Plain `node:http` has no middleware chain, so bind loopback explicitly and compose the `Host`/`Origin` guards in front of the transport ... `if (!validateHost(req, res) || !validateOrigin(req, res)) return;`". server.js:207-230 contains no such call; curl with `Origin: http://evil.example` on initialize returned 200.
```

</details>

### F-126 · [low] Fingerprint date is a locale display string (Arabic-Indic digits, embedded U+200F marks) that is inconsistent with seeds and unsortable

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/contracts.mjs`, `lib/api/client.ts`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:253 (`date: input.date || new Date().toLocaleDateString("ar-SA")`); engine/memory.mjs:25,43,60 seed dates; engine/contracts.mjs:64 (`date: z.string()`); lib/api/client.ts:67 (same pattern client-side)

The stored `date` depends on the server's ICU build and locale defaults and contains RIGHT-TO-LEFT MARK characters, so it cannot be sorted, filtered or compared, and it differs in format from the seeds ("18 سبتمبر 2026" vs "١٦‏/١٠‏/٢٠٢٦"). Any caller may also pass an arbitrary `date` string. A rendering concern (display locale) is baked into persisted data, and an English-UI viewer sees Arabic-Indic digits.

**Proposed fix:** Persist `createdAt: new Date().toISOString()` (and keep `date` only as a derived field for backward compatibility), format in the UI with Intl.DateTimeFormat using the active ar/en locale, and update FingerprintSchema accordingly; migrate seeds to ISO dates.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
`node -e 'new Date().toLocaleDateString("ar-SA")'` -> "١٦‏/١٠‏/٢٠٢٦" with char codes 661 666 200f 2f 661 660 200f 2f 662 660 662 666 (U+200F present). Seed date in the same list: "18 سبتمبر 2026". storeFingerprint result for `{}`: date '١٦‏/١٠‏/٢٠٢٦'.
```

</details>

### F-127 · [low] searchFingerprints does not trim or normalise Arabic (hamza/diacritics), so common spellings return nothing

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** i18n · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `connectors/service.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:266-283 searchFingerprints (`(query || "").toLowerCase()` ... `.join(" ").toLowerCase().includes(q)`); connectors/service.mjs:161-164

The search lowercases both sides and does a substring test. Arabic users routinely type alef without hamza and omit tashkeel; the invariant labels are stored with hamza (أداة إرسال). A query of "اداة" (no hamza) finds nothing although two seeds contain "أداة". The query is not trimmed, so a copy-pasted id with a leading space misses. toLowerCase() without a locale is also a no-op for Arabic, so the only normalisation in place helps Latin ids only.

**Proposed fix:** Add a normalize() helper: trim, NFKC, strip tashkeel (ً-ْ, ٰ), unify alef forms (أ إ آ -> ا), ة -> ه and ى -> ي, remove tatweel (ـ), then toLocaleLowerCase(); apply it to the query and to each haystack field. Reuse the same helper in telemetry.listEvents.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe: `searchFingerprints("اداة","orgS").length` -> 0; `searchFingerprints("أداة","orgS").length` -> 2; `searchFingerprints(" X-CFS-001","orgS").length` -> 0.
```

</details>

### F-128 · [low] Legacy-state migration (ensureOrgs) is duplicated in memory.mjs and telemetry.mjs with divergent behaviour; DEFAULT_ORG literal repeated in 8 places

- **Lens:** memory-matching · **Category:** hygiene · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/memory.mjs`, `engine/telemetry.mjs`, `engine/gateway.mjs`, `engine/ops-agent.mjs`, `engine/sermg.mjs`, `connectors/service.mjs`, `connectors/sdk.mjs`, `server.js`
- **Location:** engine/memory.mjs:68-82 ensureOrgs vs engine/telemetry.mjs:27-39 ensureOrgs; "demo" literals at memory.mjs:6, telemetry.mjs:5, gateway.mjs:145, ops-agent.mjs:120, sermg.mjs:58, service.mjs:25, sdk.mjs:18, server.js:17

Two copies of the flat-to-orgs migration exist: memory.mjs clears the legacy keys (`writeState({ orgs, fingerprints: undefined, sessionEvents: undefined })`) while telemetry.mjs writes `{ orgs }` only, so which module touches state first decides whether legacy keys survive. The default org id is a repeated string literal rather than a single exported constant (memory.mjs even exports DEFAULT_ORG_ID but nobody imports it). A strict reviewer flags this as duplicated state logic that can drift.

**Proposed fix:** Move ensureOrgs/orgBucket into engine/persist.mjs (one `getOrgBucket(orgId)` that migrates once and clears legacy keys) and import it from memory.mjs and telemetry.mjs; export `DEFAULT_ORG_ID` from persist.mjs and use it everywhere instead of the literal.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
grep: `engine/telemetry.mjs:37: writeState({ orgs })` vs `engine/memory.mjs:80: writeState({ orgs, fingerprints: undefined, sessionEvents: undefined })`; `grep -rn '"demo"' engine connectors server.js` lists 8 definitions; `export { MATCH_THRESHOLD, DEFAULT_ORG as DEFAULT_ORG_ID }` at memory.mjs:285 has no importers.
```

### F-129 · [low] Investigate auto-demo effect disables exhaustive-deps and runs a multi-second un-cancellable loop that keeps mutating state after navigation

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/investigate-view.tsx`
- **Location:** components/causaseal/investigate-view.tsx:167-196 runSaifDemo() and the `[params]` effect with `// eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`

runSaifDemo awaits three analyses with 700ms sleeps, calling setDemoStep/setResult/form.reset/toast and possibly createFingerprint on each step. Nothing checks for unmount, so leaving /investigate mid-demo (sidebar click) keeps firing toasts and writes fingerprints after the page is gone. The eslint-disable hides the stale-closure dependency; React 19.2 (installed: react 19.2.6 exports useEffectEvent) provides the sanctioned fix. `demoStarted` is never reset, so `/investigate?demo=1` cannot be re-triggered via URL without a full reload.

**Proposed fix:** Wrap the starter in `const startDemo = React.useEffectEvent(() => { void runSaifDemo() })` and remove the eslint-disable. Add an `aliveRef` set false in an unmount cleanup and check it after each await (skip setState/toast/save when false). Reset `demoStarted` when `params.get("demo") !== "1"`.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
investigate-view.tsx:190-196: `React.useEffect(() => { if (params.get("demo") === "1" && !demoStarted.current) { demoStarted.current = true; void runSaifDemo() } // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps }, [params])`; :179 `await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 700))` inside the loop with no cancellation check. `grep -n useEffectEvent node_modules/react/cjs/react.development.js` → exported.
```

</details>

### F-130 · [low] Object URL revoked synchronously after click in both download handlers

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** bug · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/investigate-view.tsx`, `components/causaseal/reports-view.tsx`
- **Location:** investigate-view.tsx:208-212 handleDownload; reports-view.tsx:145-149 downloadReport

Both handlers create a Blob URL, call `a.click()` and immediately `URL.revokeObjectURL(a.href)`. Firefox and Safari start the download asynchronously; revoking in the same task can abort it (empty or failed download). The anchor is also never attached to the document, which Firefox historically requires for programmatic clicks.

**Proposed fix:** Extract one `downloadText(filename, content, mime)` helper in lib/ that appends the anchor to document.body, clicks, removes it, and revokes the URL in `setTimeout(() => URL.revokeObjectURL(url), 0)` (or on the next animation frame). Use it from both views.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
investigate-view.tsx:208-212: `const a = document.createElement("a"); a.href = URL.createObjectURL(new Blob([content], …)); a.download = "CAUSASEAL_Incident_Report.txt"; a.click(); URL.revokeObjectURL(a.href)`; identical sequence in reports-view.tsx:145-149.
```

</details>

### F-131 · [low] useForm resolver cast hides zod input/output mismatch and defaultValues duplicate the DEMO constant

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `components/causaseal/investigate-view.tsx`, `engine/contracts.mjs`
- **Location:** investigate-view.tsx:112-125 `useForm<IncidentInput>({ resolver: zodResolver(IncidentInputSchema) as Resolver<IncidentInput>, defaultValues: {…} })`

IncidentInputSchema has `untrustedContent: z.string().optional().default("")` and optional `environment`, so zod's input type differs from `z.infer` (output). The `as Resolver<IncidentInput>` cast silences that instead of typing the form with input/output generics, and `environment` ends up `string | undefined` which is why the Select needs `field.value || "enterprise"` (:348). The ten-line defaultValues literal is a byte-for-byte copy of `DEMO` (:60-69).

**Proposed fix:** `useForm<z.input<typeof IncidentInputSchema>, unknown, IncidentInput>({ resolver: zodResolver(IncidentInputSchema), defaultValues: DEMO })` (react-hook-form 7.85 supports the TTransformedValues generic) and drop the cast and the duplicated literal.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
engine/contracts.mjs:8-21 `untrustedContent: z.string().optional().default("")`, `environment: z.enum([…]).optional()`. investigate-view.tsx:113 `resolver: zodResolver(IncidentInputSchema) as Resolver<IncidentInput>`; :114-124 defaultValues equal DEMO at :60-69.
```

</details>

### F-132 · [low] API client swallows all fingerprint errors into a localStorage shadow store with locale-dependent dates

- **Lens:** react-views-runtime · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `lib/api/client.ts`, `engine/memory.mjs`
- **Location:** lib/api/client.ts:34-45 fetchFingerprints, :47-75 createFingerprint; engine/memory.mjs:253

Both functions catch every error (including HTTP 500 from readJson) and silently switch to a browser-local copy of fingerprints, so server failures are hidden and the memory page can show a per-browser list that diverges from the org's real memory. `JSON.parse(raw)` is unguarded. The fallback id `X-CFS-00n` can collide with server ids once the server is reachable again. Dates use `toLocaleDateString("ar-SA")`, which depends on the ICU build and yields Arabic-Indic digits with bidi marks, inconsistent with the seeds' `18 سبتمبر 2026` — the engine has the same call, so stored fingerprints render as `١٦‏/١٠‏/٢٠٢٦` next to seeds in a different format.

**Proposed fix:** Only fall back on network failure (`error instanceof TypeError`), surface HTTP errors to the caller, guard JSON.parse, and prefix fallback ids (e.g. `LOCAL-…`) or drop the shadow store entirely since Hostinger serves API and UI from one origin. Format dates deterministically in one place (engine) with `new Intl.DateTimeFormat("ar", { calendar: "gregory", day: "numeric", month: "long", year: "numeric" })` or store ISO and format in the view.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
client.ts:38-44 `catch { const raw = localStorage.getItem("causaseal_fingerprints"); if (raw) { return { fingerprints: JSON.parse(raw) as Fingerprint[], illustrative: true } } … }`; :69 `date: input.date || new Date().toLocaleDateString("ar-SA")`. POST /api/fingerprints on the test server returned `"date":"١٦‏/١٠‏/٢٠٢٦"` while GET listed seeds with `18 سبتمبر 2026`. `node -e 'console.log(new Date().toLocaleDateString("ar-SA"))'` → `١٦‏/١٠‏/٢٠٢٦`.
```

</details>

### F-133 · [low] Stored-English users get a flash of Arabic/RTL on every full page load

### F-135 · [low] connectors/sdk.mjs is not a standalone client: importing it loads the whole server engine (gateway, memory, telemetry, persist, sermg, compliance, fixtures) just to reach redactSensitive

- **Lens:** sdk-client · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no

- **Location:** connectors/sdk.mjs:7 `import { redactSensitive } from "./service.mjs"`

The SDK is presented as 'a function inside the partner agent' / 'Repo file: connectors/sdk.mjs'. In practice it cannot be copied into a partner's agent: `redactSensitive` is imported from service.mjs, which imports compliance, gateway, memory, ops-agent, sermg and telemetry, which import persist.mjs (file-system state) and the demo fixtures. A partner process therefore links the server-side engine, its OPENAI_API_KEY branch and a disk-persistence module into a client that only needs a 10-line string-replacement helper. CLAUDE.md describes it as 'borrows only redactSensitive', which is true at the symbol level but not at the module-graph level.

**Proposed fix:** Either inline the redaction note builder in sdk.mjs (it is a pure function of the incident and an optional cut), or move `redactSensitive` into a dependency-free module (e.g. engine/redact.mjs) imported by both ops-agent.mjs and sdk.mjs. Better still, consume `redactedBody` from the server once the intercept route returns it (see the interceptTool finding) so the SDK has zero engine imports.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Loader-hook run `node --import register.mjs -e "await import('connectors/sdk.mjs')"` logged project modules: connectors/sdk.mjs, connectors/service.mjs, engine/ops-agent.mjs, engine/gateway.mjs, engine/memory.mjs, engine/telemetry.mjs, engine/persist.mjs, engine/xcfs.mjs, engine/sermg.mjs, engine/compliance.mjs, engine/fixtures/novel-leak.mjs (11 modules). Import is lazy w.r.t. disk (persist.readState is only called on use), and import time measured ~17 ms, so the cost is coupling, not performance.
```

</details>

### F-136 · [low] SDK default baseUrl is :4000 but `npm start` (production server.js) listens on :3000 by default

- **Lens:** sdk-client · **Category:** docs · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `connectors/sdk.mjs`, `server.js`, `README.md`
- **Location:** connectors/sdk.mjs:9 DEFAULT_BASE; server.js:69 `parseInt(process.env.PORT || "3000", 10)`; README.md:36-42

`DEFAULT_BASE = "http://127.0.0.1:4000"` matches the dev helper (scripts/dev.mjs sets PORT=4000 for server.js). A partner following README's `npm start` (`node server.js`) gets the API on :3000, so `beforeTool(incident)` with no baseUrl throws 'make sure server.js is running' even though it is. Minor, but it is the first thing a judge trying the SDK locally will hit, and the error message gives no hint that the port differs by script.

**Proposed fix:** Document the two ports in the SDK JSDoc and page, or read `process.env.CAUSASEAL_BASE_URL` as the default, and include the attempted URL plus 'dev API is :4000, `npm start` is :3000 unless PORT is set' in the unreachable error.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
sdk.mjs:9 `const DEFAULT_BASE = "http://127.0.0.1:4000"`; server.js:69 `const port = parseInt(process.env.PORT || "3000", 10)`; scripts/dev.mjs:13 `PORT: process.env.API_PORT || "4000"`; README.md:36 'npm run dev # الواجهة :3000 + الـ API :4000'. Probe against a closed port produced the generic 'تأكد أن server.js يعمل' message.
```

</details>

### F-137 · [low] Connectors hub HTTP card counts SDK intercepts (interceptCount is channel-agnostic) and the SDK page's 'Redacted' stat counts INTERVENE decisions, not redacted sends

- **Lens:** sdk-client · **Category:** ui · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `lib/connectors.ts`, `components/causaseal/connectors-view.tsx`, `engine/telemetry.mjs`, `components/causaseal/connectors-sdk-view.tsx`
- **Location:** lib/connectors.ts:26 countKey 'intercept'; components/causaseal/connectors-view.tsx:24-28 hubCount; engine/telemetry.mjs:186 interceptCount, 199-207 connectorSdk

The HTTP hub card uses `session.interceptCount`, which is `events.filter(source === 'intercept')` across all channels, while the MCP and SDK cards use per-channel counts. An org that only ever used the SDK shows a non-zero HTTP card, so SDK traffic is double-counted in the hub (HTTP card + SDK card). Separately, `connectorSdk.redacted` counts events labelled INTERVENE or DELIVERED-REDACTED, but the SDK never reports what it actually delivered (redaction happens client-side), so the 'Redacted' tile on /connectors/sdk is really 'INTERVENE decisions'. Judges comparing the three pages' numbers will see HTTP ≠ channels.http.

**Proposed fix:** Point the HTTP hub card at `connectorHttp.intercepts` (already channel-scoped) or make `interceptCount` channel-scoped; rename the SDK tile to 'Intervene' (ar: 'تدخّل') or have the SDK optionally report delivery outcome.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
After three SDK-only calls for orgId 'sdk-probe', `GET /api/session?orgId=sdk-probe` → `channels: {"http":0,"mcp":0,"sdk":3} | interceptCount: 3 | connectorHttp: {"intercepts":0,…} | connectorSdk: {"total":3,"allow":1,"redacted":1,"verify":1}`. hubCount in connectors-view.tsx:26 `if (key === "intercept") return session.interceptCount ?? 0`; telemetry.mjs:186 `interceptCount: events.filter((event) => event.source === "intercept").length` (no channel filter); telemetry.mjs:201-205 redacted = label INTERVENE || DELIVERED-REDACTED.
```

</details>

### F-138 · [low] /healthz discloses the server's absolute filesystem path

- **Lens:** secrets-env-logging · **Category:** security · **Breaking fix:** yes
- **Files:** `server.js`
- **Location:** server.js:233-240 /healthz handler

The unauthenticated health endpoint returns staticRoot, the absolute on-disk directory of the deployment. On Hostinger this reveals the account home and domain layout (e.g. /home/uNNN/domains/<host>/public_html/out), which is low-value for an attacker but exactly the kind of information disclosure an automated reviewer flags on a security product.

**Proposed fix:** Return `{ ok: true, hasExport, version: pkg.version }` and drop staticRoot (or return only path.basename(staticRoot), e.g. 'out'). Keep the full path in the startup console.log, which is operator-only.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
server.js:234-239 `sendJson(res, 200, { ok: true, staticRoot, hasExport: ..., hasDashboard: ... })`. Ran locally: `curl -sS http://127.0.0.1:4121/healthz` -> `{"ok":true,"staticRoot":"/home/user/causaseal/out","hasExport":true,"hasDashboard":false}`.
```

</details>

### F-139 · [low] .env.example is incomplete and implies a .env file that nothing loads for server.js

- **Lens:** secrets-env-logging · **Category:** docs · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `.env.example`, `server.js`, `engine/persist.mjs`, `scripts/dev.mjs`, `.gitignore`, `README.md`
- **Location:** .env.example:1-9; server.js:70; engine/persist.mjs:11; scripts/dev.mjs:13,17

Product code reads six environment variables but .env.example documents only NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE, OPENAI_API_KEY, OPENAI_MODEL and PORT; HOST (bind address), CAUSASEAL_STATE_PATH (state-file override used by every test) and API_PORT/WEB_PORT (dev ports) are undocumented. More importantly, no dotenv dependency and no `--env-file` flag exist, so a developer who copies .env.example to .env and sets OPENAI_API_KEY will see no effect on `npm start` or on the API process spawned by `npm run dev` (Next loads .env only into its own process; dev.mjs spawns server.js with the shell environment). The .gitignore also ignores only the single filename data/causaseal-state.json, so any alternate state file placed under data/ would be committed.

**Proposed fix:** Document every variable in .env.example with one-line comments (HOST, CAUSASEAL_STATE_PATH, API_PORT, WEB_PORT, OPENAI_TIMEOUT_MS if added). Either state explicitly in .env.example and README that server variables must be exported in the shell or the hosting panel, or make `start`/`dev` use `node --env-file-if-exists=.env server.js` (Node >=20.12/22) so the file actually works. Change the .gitignore rule to `data/*.json`.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
All process.env reads in product files: `engine/gateway.mjs:63 OPENAI_API_KEY`, `:110 OPENAI_MODEL`, `engine/persist.mjs:11 CAUSASEAL_STATE_PATH`, `server.js:69 PORT`, `server.js:70 HOST`, `scripts/dev.mjs:13 API_PORT`, `:17 WEB_PORT`, `lib/api/base.ts:4 NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE`. .env.example lists only `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE=`, `# OPENAI_API_KEY=`, `# OPENAI_MODEL=gpt-5-mini`, `# PORT=3000`. `grep -rnE 'dotenv|--env-file|loadEnvConfig' <tracked files>` returns nothing; package.json `"start": "node server.js"`, `"dev": "node scripts/dev.mjs"`; dev.mjs:11-15 `spawn(process.execPath, ["server.js"], { env: { ...process.env, PORT: process.env.API_PORT || "4000" } })`. .gitignore:25 `data/causaseal-state.json`.
```

</details>

### F-140 · [low] Unused upstream scaffold auth/env residue remains in the public repo (ChatGPT sign-in helpers, Sites/Wrangler env vars, scaffold package name)

- **Lens:** secrets-env-logging · **Category:** hygiene · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `app/chatgpt-auth.ts`, `README.md`, `.openai/hosting.json`, `scripts/sites-env.mjs`, `scripts/install-ci.sh`, `scripts/pnpm-install.mjs`, `vite.config.ts`, `build/sites-vite-plugin.ts`, `package.json`

The repository still carries the OpenAI Sites starter's identity and environment plumbing, which is misleading on a security product that judges will read: app/chatgpt-auth.ts trusts unauthenticated `oai-authenticated-user-*` request headers as identity and is imported nowhere (and `next/headers` cannot run under `output: "export"`); the README instructs readers to trust those headers; scripts read a dozen non-product env vars (SITES_INSTALL_REPORT_PATH, SITES_RUNTIME_ROOT, WRANGLER_*, MINIFLARE_REGISTRY_PATH, CLOUDFLARE_CF_FETCH_ENABLED, CODEX_SANDBOX); a mock login identity `local_seedy`/`seedy@sites.test` ships in build/sites-vite-plugin.ts and README; and package.json is still named `site-creator-vinext-starter`. None of it is on the product path (CLAUDE.md says so), but an AI or human reviewer grepping for auth will land on it first.

**Proposed fix:** Delete app/chatgpt-auth.ts, .openai/hosting.json and the Sites-only scripts/vite plugin (or move them to an `upstream-scaffold/` folder excluded from tsconfig/eslint), cut the README after the CAUSASEAL section and park the scaffold notes in docs/upstream-scaffold.md, and rename package.json to `causaseal`. Verify with npm test, npx tsc --noEmit and npm run build.

```text
`grep -rn 'chatgpt-auth' --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx --include=*.mjs --include=*.js .` (excluding node_modules/out/.next) returns no importers; app/chatgpt-auth.ts:1 `import { headers } from "next/headers";` while next.config.ts:4 sets `output: "export"`. README.md:150-152 'Signed-in visitors receive both `oai-authenticated-user-id` and `oai-authenticated-user-email`...'. Env reads: scripts/sites-env.mjs:6-19, vite.config.ts:13,40-48, scripts/install-ci.sh:195, scripts/pnpm-install.mjs:79. build/sites-vite-plugin.ts:8-9 `const localUserId = "local_seedy"; const localEmail = "seedy@sites.test";`. package.json:2 `"name": "site-creator-vinext-starter"`.
```

### F-141 · [low] electron/package.json ships placeholder contact email, wrong repository URL and an unrelated version

- **Lens:** secrets-env-logging · **Category:** hygiene · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `electron/package.json`
- **Location:** electron/package.json:4,8,12

The desktop shell manifest advertises `contact@causaseal.sa` (a domain referenced nowhere else in the repo and not shown to be owned), points `repository.url` at `github.com/aboalrejal-ai/causaseal` (a typo; the real remote is `aboalrejalai/causaseal`) and declares version 1.0.0 while the product is 0.10.10. These are the fields electron-builder embeds into installers and GitHub release metadata, so the inconsistencies would surface in any packaged artefact and look like fabricated contact information to a careful judge.

**Proposed fix:** Fix the repository URL to https://github.com/aboalrejalai/causaseal, replace the email with a real monitored address or remove the author.email field, and either align the version with the root package.json (or document that the shell is versioned independently). Optionally add a changelog rule reminder to bump both.


### F-142 · [low] paginate silently maps limit 0/NaN/non-numeric to 20, returns fractional and Infinity cursors, and throws on non-array input; only the MCP schema stands between callers and these semantics

- **Lens:** service-layer · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `connectors/service.mjs`
- **Location:** connectors/service.mjs:49-63 paginate (:50 `Math.min(50, Math.max(1, Number(limit) || 20))`, :51 `Math.max(0, Number(offset) || 0)`)

`Number(limit) || 20` treats 0, NaN, "abc", [] and "0" as "use default" rather than as invalid, so a caller asking for zero rows gets twenty; negative limits become 1 instead of an error; a fractional limit/offset is accepted and the fractional offset is echoed back as `offset: 2.5, next_offset: 4.5`, which a client will send back as the next cursor; `offset: Infinity` serialises as `null` in JSON; `items` is not checked so a non-array throws a raw TypeError. Through MCP the JSON schema rejects these (confirmed), but paginate is an exported, tested public helper and the HTTP layer is expected (per the DRY finding) to start using it, at which point these semantics become reachable.

**Proposed fix:** Normalise with integers: `const n = Number(limit); const safeLimit = Number.isFinite(n) && n >= 1 ? Math.min(50, Math.trunc(n)) : DEFAULT_LIMIT;` and `const o = Number(offset); const safeOffset = Number.isFinite(o) && o > 0 ? Math.trunc(o) : 0;` (or throw an Arabic+English validation error for out-of-range values so HTTP can return 400); guard `Array.isArray(items)`; export `DEFAULT_LIMIT`/`MAX_LIMIT` and reuse them in the MCP LIMIT schema so the two cannot drift. Extend the paginate unit test with these cases.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
service-layer-edge.mjs: `limit=0 -> count 5 (all items, i.e. default 20)`, `limit=-5 -> count 1`, `limit=NaN -> count 5`, `limit=abc -> count 5`, `limit="0" -> count 5`, `limit=2.5 -> count 2`, `limit=2 offset=2.5 -> {"count":2,"offset":2.5,"next_offset":4.5}`, `limit=2 offset=Infinity -> {"count":0,"offset":null,...}`, `limit=true -> count 1`, `paginate(non-array) throws: TypeError items.slice is not a function`. MCP schema does reject: `{"limit":0} -> data/limit must be >= 1`, `{"limit":2.5} -> data/limit must be integer`.
```

</details>

### F-143 · [low] Every paginated page returns its rows twice (items plus events/fingerprints/controls), doubling JSON payloads and confusing the API shape

- **Lens:** service-layer · **Category:** api · **Breaking fix:** yes
- **Files:** `connectors/service.mjs`, `connectors/mcp-server.mjs`
- **Location:** connectors/service.mjs:147-152 `{ ...page, events: page.items, ... }`, :166-171 `{ ...page, fingerprints: page.items, ... }`, :233-237 `{ ...page, controls: page.items, ... }`

paginate returns `{ items, total_count, count, offset, has_more, next_offset }`; each list function spreads it and then adds a second key pointing at the same array. Clients therefore receive both `items` and the named key with identical content, the JSON response_format output is twice as large as necessary (and reaches the 25 000-char truncation at the documented limit=50), and consumers cannot tell which key is canonical (the markdown renderers read `events`/`fingerprints`/`controls`, the tests read `items`).

**Proposed fix:** Return one named collection per tool: destructure `const { items, ...meta } = paginate(...)` and return `{ ...meta, events: items, illustrative, orgId }` (same for fingerprints/controls); update test/connectors-service.test.mjs which reads `page.items` only for the raw paginate helper (unchanged). Mention the shape in the tool descriptions.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
service-layer-edge.mjs: `keys: items,total_count,count,offset,has_more,fingerprints,illustrative,orgId | items===fingerprints: true`, `JSON size with items dup: 5818 without: 2970`; over /mcp `structured has both items & events: true` on a 36 686-char page.
```

</details>

### F-144 · [low] Validation and guidance strings are Arabic-only and toolError forwards raw engine TypeErrors, breaking the bilingual reply convention and leaking internals

- **Lens:** service-layer · **Category:** i18n · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `connectors/service.mjs`, `connectors/mcp-server.mjs`
- **Location:** connectors/service.mjs:28-29 ORG_ID_ERROR, :191 hint, :279-286 guidance, :328 title error; connectors/mcp-server.mjs:107-113 toolError

AGENTS.md requires MCP descriptions and markdown replies to be Arabic then English, and every tool description complies, but the strings a client actually acts on are Arabic-only (`orgId يقبل حروفًا...`, `title مطلوب...`, `لا ترسل الأصل. قُطعت: ...`, `شغّل causaseal_run_agent ثم أعد القراءة...`), prefixed by an English `Error:`. An English-only agent (or judge) cannot read why a call failed or what guidance to follow. toolError also passes through unexpected engine exceptions verbatim, so clients see `Error: current is not iterable` or `nodes.map is not a function` instead of a stable, bilingual message.

**Proposed fix:** Make ORG_ID_ERROR, the title error, guidance and hint bilingual (Arabic sentence then English sentence, matching the descriptions). Introduce a small `ServiceError` class (with a `code` such as INVALID_ORG_ID / TITLE_REQUIRED) thrown by the service; in toolError, format ServiceError messages as-is and map any other exception to a generic bilingual "خطأ داخلي / Internal error" while logging the original server-side. Update test/connectors-service.test.mjs expectations.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
service-layer-edge.mjs section M: `orgId error: orgId يقبل حروفًا وأرقامًا و _ و - حتى 64، أو اتركه فارغًا ليُستخدم demo`, `title error: title مطلوب. مرّر عنوان البصمة ثم أعد المحاولة.`, `intercept guidance: لا ترسل الأصل. قُطعت: تعليمة في النص المسترجع. استخدم redactedBody فقط.`, `report hint: شغّل causaseal_run_agent ثم أعد القراءة بنفس orgId.`; over /mcp: `get_session __proto__ -> isError: true | text: "Error: current is not iterable"`. AGENTS.md: "Descriptions and markdown replies: Arabic then English."
```

</details>

### F-145 · [low] Channel whitelist is copy-pasted six times and MCP-WRITE telemetry blocks twice; listEventsPage lacks the type guard its sibling listFingerprintsPage has

- **Lens:** service-layer · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `connectors/service.mjs`, `engine/ops-agent.mjs`, `engine/gateway.mjs`
- **Location:** connectors/service.mjs:246-248, :296-298, :312-314 (`["http","mcp","sdk"].includes(String(x.channel)) ? String(x.channel) : "http"`), :330-347 and :359-376 (identical recordEvent blocks differing only in tool/path), :141-145 listEventsPage vs :161 listFingerprintsPage; engine/ops-agent.mjs:83-85, :122-124; engine/gateway.mjs:146-148

The same three-line channel normalisation appears six times across three files and the same 15-line MCP-WRITE recordEvent literal appears twice in service.mjs next to noteMcpRead, which already encapsulates the MCP-READ variant. Beyond style, the duplication is where drift happens (runSermg already forgot to forward channel). listEventsPage passes `params.q || ""` and `params.status || "all"` straight to telemetry.listEvents, whereas listFingerprintsPage guards `typeof params.q === "string"`; a non-string q therefore throws `TypeError: filter.q.toLowerCase is not a function` and an unknown status silently yields zero rows. The MCP schema prevents this today, but the service is also the intended call surface for server.js and tests.

**Proposed fix:** Export `CHANNELS = ["http","mcp","sdk"]` and `normalizeChannel(value)` from engine/telemetry.mjs (or a small engine/channel.mjs) and use it in service.mjs, ops-agent.mjs and gateway.mjs; add `noteMcpWrite(toolName, path, orgId)` beside noteMcpRead and call it from storeFingerprintItem and runSermg. In listEventsPage coerce `q` with `typeof params.q === "string" ? params.q : ""` and validate `status` against ["all","blocked","verify","allowed"] (export the list and reuse it in the MCP schema).

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Grep of the literal `["http", "mcp", "sdk"].includes(` yields service.mjs:246, :296, :312; ops-agent.mjs:83, :122; gateway.mjs:146. service.mjs:331-346 and :360-375 are identical apart from `tool`/`path`. service-layer-edge.mjs: `listEventsPage({orgId:"demo", q: 5})` → `q=5 throws: TypeError filter.q.toLowerCase is not a function`; `listFingerprintsPage({orgId:"demo", q: 5})` → `ok (guarded)`; `status='bogus' -> 0`.
```

</details>

### F-146 · [low] retrievedTextOf coerces non-string untrustedContent with String(), so arrays/objects become analysable text

- **Lens:** xcfs-invariants · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/xcfs.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/xcfs.mjs:19-24 retrievedTextOf (`return String(body.untrustedContent ?? "")`)

When retrievedText is missing/empty the fallback stringifies whatever untrustedContent is: an array joins with commas and can carry a directive verb, an object becomes '[object Object]', a number becomes digits. Combined with the absent schema validation on the HTTP routes, a malformed body is analysed as if it were text instead of being rejected.

**Proposed fix:** Accept only strings: `const r = body.retrievedText; if (typeof r === "string" && r.trim()) return r; return typeof body.untrustedContent === "string" ? body.untrustedContent : ""`. With schema validation at the boundary this becomes defence in depth.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe I: retrievedTextOf({untrustedContent:{a:1}}) → "[object Object]"; retrievedTextOf({untrustedContent:["انقل","x"]}) → "انقل,x" (which DIRECTIVE then matches); retrievedTextOf({retrievedText:123, untrustedContent:"fallback"}) → "fallback".
```

</details>

### F-147 · [low] SEND_TOOLS membership is exact and case/whitespace-sensitive in both the engine and the harness

- **Lens:** xcfs-invariants · **Category:** quality · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `engine/xcfs.mjs`, `engine/ops-agent.mjs`
- **Location:** engine/xcfs.mjs:6-11 SEND_TOOLS, :42-43 (`SEND_TOOLS.has(String(body.tool || ""))`); engine/ops-agent.mjs:26-27 harnessDecision

'Send_To_Workspace', ' send_to_workspace' or 'send_to_workspace ' are not recognised as send tools, so 'أداة إرسال' is silently dropped and fallback()'s sendLeak/directedSend rules (which require it) cannot fire; the harness makes the same mistake, so the compare row shows ALLOW/VERIFY for what is the known leak shape. The engine intentionally handles tools outside SEND_TOOLS via the destination invariant, so this is a robustness gap rather than a bypass, but a strict reviewer will expect one normalised tool identifier. (Separately, 'share_document' and 'invoke_vendor_api' appear in SEND_TOOLS but nowhere else in fixtures, UI, SERMG or docs.)

**Proposed fix:** Add `export function normalizeTool(tool) { return String(tool ?? "").trim().toLowerCase() }` in xcfs.mjs and use it in deriveInvariants, expand (node value) and harnessDecision; add a one-line test. Decide whether share_document/invoke_vendor_api should be exercised by a fixture or removed.

<details><summary>Evidence (as captured by the auditor)</summary>

```text
Probe K: deriveInvariants({...NOVEL_LEAK, tool: t}).includes('أداة إرسال') → true for "send_to_workspace", false for "Send_To_Workspace", " send_to_workspace", "send_to_workspace ". grep for share_document/invoke_vendor_api outside engine/xcfs.mjs → only engine/sermg.mjs:9 TOOLS (invoke_vendor_api).
```

</details>

### F-148 · [low] Radar axis 'نداء سابق' is a step-node label, never an invariant, so it is permanently zero on /investigate

- **Lens:** xcfs-invariants · **Category:** ui · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `lib/chart-session.ts`, `engine/xcfs.mjs`
- **Location:** lib/chart-session.ts:60-67 INVARIANT_AXES, :90-97 INVARIANT_SHORT; engine/xcfs.mjs:49-59 deriveInvariants, :74 (step node label 'نداء سابق')

INVARIANT_AXES mixes the five real invariant identifiers with 'نداء سابق', which deriveInvariants() never emits (it is the label expand() gives to pruned step nodes, and memory.graphSimilarity explicitly filters it out). invariantsToRadar therefore always draws that axis at 0, and INVARIANT_SHORT carries a dead mapping. Node labelling between engine and UI is otherwise consistent.

**Proposed fix:** Remove 'نداء سابق' from INVARIANT_AXES/INVARIANT_SHORT, or export the invariant identifiers from the engine (e.g. `INVARIANT_NAMES` in xcfs.mjs re-exported through lib/contracts.ts) so the UI cannot drift from the engine's label set.

### F-149 · [low] No direct unit tests for xcfs.mjs helpers (hasDirectiveInRetrieved, retrievedTextOf, expand, reduce)

- **Lens:** xcfs-invariants · **Category:** test · **Breaking fix:** no
- **Files:** `test/gateway.test.mjs`, `engine/xcfs.mjs`
- **Location:** test/ (no test imports hasDirectiveInRetrieved/retrievedTextOf/expand/reduce); engine/xcfs.mjs

The only coverage of the engine's core invariant derivation is indirect: gateway.test.mjs calls deriveInvariants on the five fixtures. None of the defects above (substring false positives, prompt suppression, normalisation, string flags, steps-driven ratio, missing trustedDestination) is guarded by a test, which is why they survived into a tagged release that judges will read.

**Proposed fix:** Add test/xcfs.test.mjs covering: false-positive words with trusted destination → no directive invariant; same-verb vs any-verb suppression; tashkeel/tatweel/bare-alef/zero-width evasions → detected; string-typed flags rejected at the boundary (or ignored by strict checks); reductionRatio independent of steps length (or whatever formula is chosen); missing trustedDestination handling; tool-name normalisation. The probe scripts in the scratch dir (xcfs-invariants-probe.mjs, xcfs-invariants-fixcheck.mjs) contain ready-made cases.


</details>
